## AI summary

Adds feature-gated partial preemption for opted-in elastic Jobs. Kueue can reclaim quota by scaling down one eligible PodSet instead of evicting the entire Workload. The Job integration selects Pods above the target, with full eviction used when partial preemption is not suitable.

### Suggested release note

Job: Added feature-gated partial preemption for opted-in elastic Jobs, allowing Kueue to reclaim quota by scaling down a PodSet to its configured minimum instead of evicting the entire Workload.
<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->

```release-note
Introduce the Partial Preemption feature for opted-in elastic Jobs, allowing Kueue to reclaim quota by scaling down a PodSet to its configured minimum instead of evicting the entire Workload.
```

<details>
<summary><strong>Walkthrough</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>
<br>

This change adds feature-gated partial preemption for eligible elastic workloads. It introduces new API fields, scheduler target planning, Job Pod eviction, and quota release. It also adds validation, feature-gate wiring, docs, and integration tests.

**Changes: Partial preemption**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**Contracts and workload state** <br> `apis/kueue/v1beta2/workload_types.go`, `charts/kueue/templates/crd/kueue.x-k8s.io_workloads.yaml`, `config/components/crd/bases/kueue.x-k8s.io_workloads.yaml`, `pkg/features/kube_features.go`, `pkg/workload/*`, `pkg/constants/constants.go`, `site/content/en/...`, `site/data/featuregates/versioned_feature_list.yaml`, `test/compatibility_lifecycle/reference/versioned_feature_list.yaml`, `site/static/examples/jobs/*`, `pkg/util/testing/v1beta2/wrappers.go`, `pkg/config/validation.go`, `pkg/config/validation_test.go`, `pkg/webhooks/workload_webhook.go`, `pkg/webhooks/workload_webhook_test.go`|Adds the `PartialPreemption` feature gate, the `PreemptionMinCount` and `PreemptionTargetCount` fields, workload condition and helper state, CRD schemas, feature-gate validation, webhook admission checks, documentation, and a sample Job.|
|**Scheduler planning** <br> `pkg/scheduler/preemption/*`, `pkg/cache/scheduler/snapshot.go`, `pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go`, `pkg/scheduler/assignment/native/native.go`, `pkg/scheduler/flavorassigner/tas_elastic_accounting_test.go`, `pkg/workloadslicing/workloadslicing_test.go`, `pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go`|Changes candidate grouping, target planning, snapshot simulation, and ordering for partial preemption. It also updates callers and tests to use reduced workload replacements and target wrappers.|
|**Job eviction and quota reconciliation** <br> `pkg/controller/jobframework/*`, `pkg/controller/jobs/job/*`, `pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go`, `pkg/controller/elasticjobs/elastic_job_ungater.go`, `pkg/workloadslicing/manager.go`, `pkg/workload/patching/patching.go`, `charts/kueue/templates/rbac/role.yaml`, `config/components/rbac/role.yaml`|Adds Job validation and Pod selection, partial-preemption reconciliation through the eviction API, admission count and resource updates, and target clearing after count convergence. Workload-slice scale-up is deferred while a target is pending.|
|**Integration validation** <br> `test/integration/singlecluster/controller/jobs/job/*`, `test/integration/singlecluster/scheduler/preemption_test.go`|Adds integration tests for Pod draining, quota release, workload-slice behavior, and cohort preemption.|

<!-- change_assessment_start -->

**Possibly related issues**
- [kubernetes-sigs/kueue#15211](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/15211) — This issue concerns preemption planning order for workloads that are already evicted, and this PR changes classical candidate buckets and traversal.
- [kubernetes-sigs/kueue#17430](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/17430) — This follow-up requests partial-preemption support for RayJob and RayCluster integrations, which is adjacent to the new partial-preemption flow but not added here.
- [kubernetes-sigs/kueue#18163](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/18163) — This issue tracks the scheduler-library update for incremental preemption simulation, which this PR uses through reduced workload replacement logic.

<details>
<summary><strong>Full details: Linked Issues check</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>
<br>

**Explanation**

[`#975`] The PR implements partial preemption for eligible elastic workloads. It adds PodSet minimum counts, admission target counts, feature gating, target selection, pod reclamation, quota release, and workload status handling. It adds the required Job validation and integration behavior. Scheduler tests and integration tests cover partial and whole preemption paths. API schemas and API reference documentation include the new fields. The design document requirement is non-coding and does not affect this coding assessment. The latest changes also validate incompatible feature gates and correct effective-priority band handling.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Full details: Out of Scope Changes check</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>
<br>

**Explanation**

The changed API, scheduler, controller, webhook, RBAC, workload-slicing, ungating, documentation, examples, and tests support partial preemption for [`#975`]. The feature-gate compatibility validation and effective-priority fixes support the same behavior. The legacy v0.19 reference uses `reclaimTargetCount` instead of the current `preemptionTargetCount`; this is a documentation naming concern, but it remains related to the feature and is not unrelated scope. No demonstrated unrelated change appears.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

</dd>

```diff
+			if fitted {
 				targets = fillBackWorkloads(preemptionCtx, targets, attemptOpts.borrowing)
 				restoreSnapshot(preemptionCtx.snapshot, targets)
 				return targets
 			}
 		}
+		// If the current attempt cannot fit, we still perform partial preemption. Partial preemption
+		// prioritizes preempting the workload that can be partially preempted in the first attempt.
+		// If there is no workload that can be partially preempted in the first attempt, the workload
+		// that can be partially preempted in the second attempt will be used.
+		if len(partialProgress) == 0 {
+			partialProgress = partialTargets(targets)

-	return nil

+}
+
+// preemptCandidate simulates preempting the candidate in the snapshot and appends the corresponding
+// Target, then reports whether the preemptor now fits (per the fits closure).
+//
+// It prefers partial preemption: a partial-preemptible elastic candidate is scaled down to its
+// minCount (its reduced copy replaces the full workload in the snapshot) instead of being evicted
+// wholly, so the job keeps running at reduced size. Partial reductions accumulate across candidates
+// — a partial-preemptible candidate is never evicted wholly here; it is only ever whole-evicted once
+// it is no longer partial-preemptible (e.g. already scaled down to minCount in a previous cycle).
+// With the PartialPreemption gate off, partialPreemptionFor returns nil and this reduces to the
+// original whole-eviction behavior.
+func preemptCandidate(preemptionCtx *preemptionCtx, targets []*Target, candidate *workload.Info, reason string, fits func() bool) ([]*Target, bool) {
+	t := &Target{
+		WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+		Reason:       reason,
+		WorkloadCq:   preemptionCtx.snapshot.ClusterQueue(candidate.ClusterQueue),
+	}
+	if pp, reduced := partialPreemptionFor(candidate); pp != nil {
+		t.PartialPreemption = pp
+		t.reducedInfo = reduced
+	}
+	applyPreemption(preemptionCtx.snapshot, t)
+	return append(targets, t), fits()
+}
+
+// applyPreemption simulates preempting the target in the snapshot: for a partial target it replaces
+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if t.reducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.reducedInfo)
+	}
+}
+
+// undoPreemption reverses applyPreemption, restoring the full workload in the snapshot.
+func undoPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.reducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.reducedInfo)
+	}
+	snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+}
+
+// partialTargets returns the subset of targets that are partial (scale-down) preemptions.
+func partialTargets(targets []*Target) []*Target {
+	var out []*Target
+	for _, t := range targets {
+		if t.PartialPreemption != nil {
+			out = append(out, t)
+		}
+	}
+	return out
+}
+
+// partialPreemptionFor returns the partial-preemption request (scale the elastic PodSet down to
+// its minCount) and the corresponding reduced-usage Info, or (nil, nil) if the candidate is not
+// eligible for partial preemption. First version: only candidates with exactly one reclaimable
+// PodSet (e.g. the Spark executor PodSet) are handled partially; others fall back to whole
+// eviction.
+func partialPreemptionFor(candidate *workload.Info) (*PartialPreemptionTarget, *workload.Info) {
+	if !workload.IsPartialPreemptionJob(candidate.Obj) {
+		return nil, nil
+	}
+	reclaimable := workload.PartialPreemptibleCounts(candidate.Obj)
+	if len(reclaimable) != 1 {
+		return nil, nil
+	}
+	var psName kueue.PodSetReference
+	for name := range reclaimable {
+		psName = name
+	}
+	minCount, _ := workload.MinCount(candidate.Obj, psName)
+	reduced := workload.ReducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate,
```

  **Do not clear reclaim targets for legacy assignments with nil `Count`.**
  
  `PodSetAssignment.Count` is optional for older Workloads, and conversion preserves nil. Partial-preemption selection does not require this field. It can set `ReclaimTargetCount` through `SetReclaimTargetCount`.
  
  When the spec count reaches the target, `reconcilePartialPreemption` falls back to that reduced spec count. The fallback prevents `ResourceUsage` scaling, but the function still clears `ReclaimTargetCount`. Cache accounting then continues to use the original aggregate `ResourceUsage` unless `Status.ReclaimablePods` separately reports reclaimable pods. The Workload can therefore keep its original quota reservation and leave the preemptor pending.
  
  Skip partial-preemption selection for assignments with nil `Count`. Also retain the reclaim target until a non-nil admitted count is available. Add a regression test for a legacy admitted Workload with nil `PodSetAssignment.Count`.

  **Synchronize partial-preemption annotations on existing Workloads.**
  
  When reconciliation selects an existing Workload for `updateWorkloadToMatchJob`, `prepareWorkload` writes the annotations to `newWl`, but the update copies only `newWl.Spec` into `wl`. `r.client.Update` therefore preserves stale Workload metadata.
  
  A suspended Job that changes or removes either annotation can leave the Workload opted in, or leave an outdated maximum reclaim count. The scheduler reads both values from Workload annotations.
  
  Copy both annotation keys from `newWl` to `wl` before `r.client.Update`, and delete each key from `wl` when it is absent from `newWl`. Add reconciliation coverage for additions, changes, and removals.

  _🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_
  
  **Gate the pending partial-preemption branch on the feature gate.**
  
  `pendingPartialPreemptionTarget` calls `workload.ReclaimTargetCounts`, which does not check `features.PartialPreemption` (see `pkg/workload/partial_preemption.go` lines 64-76). `partialPreemptionInfoFor` does check it, through `IsPartialPreemptionJob`. So the doc comment at Line 427 ("With the PartialPreemption gate off ... this reduces to the original whole-eviction behavior") does not hold for this first branch.
  
  Trigger: a workload keeps `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount` from an earlier admission, then the gate is disabled (rollback or downgrade). `preemptCandidate` then returns a pending partial target instead of a whole-eviction target. `IssuePreemptions` issues no patch and counts the victim as preempted, but no component reduces the workload while the gate is off. The preemptor stays pending and never frees quota.
  
  Add the gate check to the pending branch.
  
  <details>
  <summary>🐛 Proposed fix</summary>
  
  ```diff
   func pendingPartialPreemptionTarget(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
  +	if !workload.IsPartialPreemptionJob(candidate.Obj) {
  +		return nil
  +	}
   	targetCounts := workload.ReclaimTargetCounts(candidate.Obj)
   	if len(targetCounts) == 0 {
   		return nil
   	}
  ```
  </details>

  ```
  minimal, and validate.
  
  In `@pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go` at line 430, Gate the pending
  partial-preemption branch in preemptCandidate by checking
  features.PartialPreemption before calling pendingPartialPreemptionTarget. When
  the gate is disabled, skip that branch and preserve the existing whole-eviction
  behavior; when enabled, retain the current pending-target handling.
  
  After applying the fix, consider running `coderabbit review --agent` for local
  review. Visit https://docs.coderabbit.ai/cli?utm_source=ghpr
  ```

  **Release partial-preemption Pods in the WAS simulator.** When `WorkloadInfoForReplacement()` reports a partial target, `target.WorkloadInfo` is omitted from `targetWorkloads`, so `SimulatorSnapshot.PreemptWorkload` is never called for that target. The WAS simulator only removes Pods for an entire workload and exposes no partial-removal operation. `FindTopologyAssignmentsForWorkload` can therefore reject a TAS assignment that fits after the scale-down because the reclaimed Pods still occupy their nodes.
  
  Add partial-Pod removal support and use it for partial targets. If that support is unavailable, reject partial preemption for TAS before selecting the target.

  ```
  In `@pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go` around lines 1042 - 1044, Update the target
  collection around WorkloadInfoForReplacement so partial targets either invoke a
  new partial-Pod removal operation during SimulatorSnapshot.PreemptWorkload, or
  are rejected for TAS before target selection when that operation is unavailable.
  Ensure reclaimed Pods are removed from the WAS simulator before
  FindTopologyAssignmentsForWorkload evaluates assignments, while preserving the
  existing full-workload path.
  ```

  **Validate `partial-preemption-max-reclaimed-count` on Workloads.** Direct Workload create and annotation-update validation accepts malformed, zero, and negative values. The parser treats these values as absent, so the scheduler skips the cap and can reclaim replicas down to `minCount`, beyond the requested limit. Reject malformed and non-positive values as positive `int32` values with a clear `field.Invalid` error.

  **Validate `ResourceUsage` before allowing convergence.**
  
  This assignment permits any divisible `ResourceUsage` value. For example, an old count of 6 with CPU usage 6 can converge to count 2 with CPU usage 100. The immutable check then accepts the modified quota accounting.
  
  Require each new resource quantity to equal the old per-pod quantity multiplied by the converged count. Reject mismatched resource keys or quantities with `field.Invalid`.
  
  As per path instructions, “Check that validating webhooks reject invalid input with clear error messages.” <path_instructions>

**Replace the fixed sleep in the timeout test.**

The integration-test guidance applies to this `*_test.go` file and requires fake clocks, hooks, or channels instead of fixed sleeps. Inject a fake clock through `scheduler.WithClock` and advance it after the initial reclaim request, or use an event-based synchronization mechanism.

The sleep is a lower bound, not an exact-deadline race: the timeout predicate remains true after the configured duration. The fixed wall-clock wait still violates the test guidance.

```diff
+// WorkloadReplacement describes a workload removal. Reduced is the workload
+// retained after partial preemption; nil means full eviction.
+type WorkloadReplacement struct {
+	Full    *workload.Info
+	Reduced *workload.Info
+}
+
+// SimulateWorkloadRemoval temporarily removes each full workload from the
+// snapshot. For partial preemption, it replaces the full workload with Reduced.
+// It returns a function that restores the original workloads and usage.
+func (s *Snapshot) SimulateWorkloadRemoval(replacements []WorkloadReplacement) func() {
+	return s.simulateWorkloadRemoval(replacements, false)
+}
+
+// SimulateWorkloadRemovalWithHiddenReplacements temporarily removes each full
+// workload from the snapshot. For partial preemption, it retains Reduced's
+// usage without exposing the reduced workload through ClusterQueue.Workloads.
+// This prevents targets already selected in the current scheduling cycle from
+// being selected again while preserving their post-preemption resource usage.
+func (s *Snapshot) SimulateWorkloadRemovalWithHiddenReplacements(replacements []WorkloadReplacement) func() {
+	return s.simulateWorkloadRemoval(replacements, true)
+}
+
```

  **Make hidden-replacement accounting match the snapshot helpers.**

  **Do not remove all Pods of a partial-preemption victim from the scheduling simulator.**
  
  `WorkloadReplacements(targets)` frees only the reclaimed slice, but `SimulatePodRemoval` calls `PreemptWorkload` for every target. The WAS simulator preempts all indexed Pods for that workload. The TAS assignment can therefore treat retained-Pod nodes as free and produce an assignment that does not fit after scale-down.
  
  Apply the conservative filter at both call sites, or add a simulator operation that removes only the reclaimed Pods.

  **Document effective-count convergence for `reclaimTargetCount`.**
  
  When `ReclaimablePods` is enabled, Kueue clears `ReclaimTargetCount` when the effective PodSet count reaches the target. The effective count subtracts reclaimable pods from the desired spec count. Therefore, a spec count of five with three reclaimable pods can satisfy a target of two without a spec update.
  
  Update the API comment, then regenerate the CRD and API reference documentation so all generated descriptions remain consistent.
  
  <details>
  <summary>Suggested API comment update</summary>
  
  ```diff
  -	// pods down to reclaimTargetCount. Kueue clears this field once the PodSet's
  -	// desired spec count has converged to reclaimTargetCount or below.
  +	// pods down to reclaimTargetCount. Kueue clears this field once the PodSet's
  +	// effective count has converged to reclaimTargetCount or below. The effective
  +	// count is reduced by reclaimable pods when the ReclaimablePods feature gate is enabled.
  ```
  
  </details>

`375-389`: **🎯 Functional Correctness** | **🟡 Minor** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Retry the partial-preemption status patch on conflict.**

With `WorkloadRequestUseMergePatch` enabled, a stale victim `resourceVersion` makes this patch return a conflict error, and the call does not retry. The whole-eviction branch passes `WithRetryOnConflict()`, but this branch does not. Add the same option. The scheduler then does not lose the scale-down request to a single concurrent status update.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-			}, patching.WithLooseOnApply()); err != nil {
+			}, patching.WithLooseOnApply(), patching.WithRetryOnConflict()); err != nil {
```

</details>

`585-593`: **📐 Maintainability & Code Quality** | **🟡 Minor** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Add assertions that the scale-down clears the reclaim target and resets Preempted.**

The first partial-preemption case is named "clear reclaimTargetCount on scale-down", but its final steps check only admission. A regression that leaves `reclaimTargetCount` set or `WorkloadPreempted` true would still pass. The inline comment also says the workload-slicing Manager clears the target and this suite does not wire it up. The inspected code shows the workload controller clears it, and this suite starts that controller. Add the two assertions after the scale-down, and remove the incorrect comment. The second partial-preemption case has the same comment and needs the same change.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-			// Lowering spec.count drops the victim's effective usage to min(spec,
-			// admission), freeing quota for the preemptor. Clearing the now-satisfied
-			// reclaimTargetCount is the workloadslicing Manager's job (driven by the
-			// job controller), which this scheduler-only suite does not wire up, so we
-			// assert on quota being freed rather than on the target being cleared.
 			ginkgo.By("Scaling the victim down to the reclaim target frees quota and admits the preemptor")
 			setResizeCount(victim, 2)
 			expectAdmittedCount(victim, 2)
+			expectReclaimTargetCount(victim, nil)
+			expectPreemptedCondition(victim, metav1.ConditionFalse)
 			behavioral.ExpectWorkloadsToBeAdmitted(ctx, k8sClient, preemptor)
```

**Add released-usage and overlapping-TAS cases for hidden replacements.**

The only test case for `SimulateWorkloadRemovalWithHiddenReplacements` uses one CPU flavor and an unreleased workload. Simulated removal with a released victim or overlapping TAS flavors is not tested through this method. A regression in those paths would not fail the suite.

`5692-5695`: **📐 Maintainability & Code Quality** | **🔵 Trivial** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Assert Completions in the non-sync cases of TestRespondToPartialPreemption.**

The test checks `Spec.Completions` only when the sync annotation is `"true"`. The scale-down case without that annotation does not check it, so a handler that sets `Completions` on every Job would pass the test. Capture the original `Completions` before the call, and assert it is unchanged unless sync is enabled.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
 			j := (*Job)(tc.job)
+			wantCompletions := tc.job.Spec.Completions
+			if tc.wantChanged && tc.job.Annotations[JobCompletionsEqualParallelismAnnotation] == "true" {
+				wantCompletions = tc.wantParallelism
+			}
 			c := utiltesting.NewClientBuilder().
```

```diff
-			if tc.wantChanged && tc.job.Annotations[JobCompletionsEqualParallelismAnnotation] == "true" {
-				if diff := cmp.Diff(tc.wantParallelism, got.Spec.Completions); diff != "" {
-					t.Errorf("Completions mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", diff)
-				}
-			}
+			if diff := cmp.Diff(wantCompletions, got.Spec.Completions); diff != "" {
+				t.Errorf("Completions mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", diff)
+			}
```

</details>

As per path instructions, the test should "cover relevant branches, important edge cases, and errors" (site/content/en/community/contribution_guidelines/writing_tests.md). The non-sync path is a relevant branch, and it is not covered.

  The field is marked `+optional` but is a `bool`, so an omitted value and an explicit `false` decode identically. Repository guidance requires pointer types for optional fields. Change the field to `*bool`, then update its consumer in `cmd/kueue/main.go` and the generated files that reference this type.

  ```diff
  +	EnableOnDemandPreemption *bool `json:"enableOnDemandPreemption,omitempty"`
  ```

  </details>

  **Clear the stale Preempted condition when an external client removes the last reclaim target.**
  
  The admission webhook lets a client with `workloads/status` update access clear `reclaimTargetCount`. When that happens, `reconcileResizeScaleDown` finds no target and returns without touching `WorkloadPreempted`. The condition stays `True` until the workload is re-admitted. The scheduler's `SetPreemptedCondition` does not refresh `LastTransitionTime` on an existing `True` condition, so the stale timestamp can later make `partialPreemptionTimedOut` fall back to full eviction.

  **Add the partial-preemption annotation only for jobs that implement `JobWithPartialPreemption`.**
  
  Other integrations copy the annotation to their Workloads. The scheduler can select these Workloads for partial preemption when they have a PodSet `minCount`, such as RayCluster with elastic partial scale-up. Only `Job` implements `RespondToPartialPreemption`. By default, the victim keeps its replicas and the preemptor waits.
  
  <details>
  <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
  <dl>
  <dd>
  
  ```diff
  -	if job.Object().GetAnnotations()[constants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation] == "true" {
  +	if _, ok := job.(JobWithPartialPreemption); ok && job.Object().GetAnnotations()[constants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation] == "true" {
   		metav1.SetMetaDataAnnotation(&wl.ObjectMeta, constants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation, "true")
   	}
  ```
  
  </dd>
  </dl>
  </details>

- **pkg/controller/jobs/job/job_controller.go:528** by [@coderabbitai[bot]](https://github.com/apps/coderabbitai) — 2026-11-03T06:44:24Z
```diff
@@ -497,6 +498,41 @@ func (j *Job) syncCompletionWithParallelism() bool {
 	return false
 }
 
+// RespondToPartialPreemption implements jobframework.JobWithPartialPreemption.
+// When the scheduler has requested a partial preemption scale-down (indicated by a
+// non-nil ReclaimTargetCount on the Workload's first PodSet assignment), this method
+// patches spec.parallelism to the requested count so the Job releases excess quota
+// without being fully evicted.
+func (j *Job) RespondToPartialPreemption(ctx context.Context, c client.Client, wl *kueue.Workload) error {
+	if j.minPodsCount() == nil {
+		// Job does not support elastic sizing; partial preemption requires the
+		// kueue.x-k8s.io/job-min-parallelism annotation.
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	targetCount, ok := workload.ReclaimTargetCount(wl, kueue.DefaultPodSetName)
+	if !ok {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	currentParallelism := ptr.Deref(j.Spec.Parallelism, 1)
+	if currentParallelism <= targetCount {
+		// Already at or below the target; nothing to do.
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	if err := clientutil.Patch(ctx, c, j.Object(), func() (bool, error) {
+		j.Spec.Parallelism = &targetCount
+		if j.syncCompletionWithParallelism() {
+			j.Spec.Completions = &targetCount
+		}
```

  **Do not lower Completions below the completed indexes during partial preemption.**
  
  When `job-completions-equal-parallelism` is set, `RespondToPartialPreemption` sets `Completions` to the reclaim target. If that target is below the number of completed indexes in range, the Job completes, and Kueue finishes its Workload before the victim runs at the reduced size.
  
  The example in the hypothesis does not reach this code: target 2 with 3 succeeded and completions 5 is not a pending reclaim. The reachable case is a target of 1 with completed indexes 0-2. A safe fix needs a design decision. Kubernetes allows `completions` changes on Indexed Jobs only while `completions` equals `parallelism`, so the handler cannot simply set `Completions` to succeeded count plus target.

  ```
  Update RespondToPartialPreemption’s Completions adjustment when
  syncCompletionWithParallelism is true so partial preemption cannot make an
  Indexed Job complete while completed indexes remain beyond the reclaim target.
  Establish an approach compatible with Kubernetes’ rule that Indexed Job
  completions may change only when equal to parallelism; do not simply set
  ```

  **Use the reclaimed quota when computing the target's old share for partial targets.**
  
  For a partial target, `verifyFairSharingTarget` adds the full workload usage to a snapshot that already holds the reduced copy. The old share is then overstated. Use `reclaimedQuotaUsage(...)` for partial targets, as the yield path already does.
  
  <details>
  <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
  <dl>
  <dd>
  
  ```diff
   	preemptorNode, targetNode := fairsharing.AlmostLCAs(preemptionCtx.preemptorCQ, t.WorkloadCq)
   	preemptorShare := fairsharing.PreemptorNewShare(preemptorNode.DominantResourceShare())
   	newShare := fairsharing.TargetNewShare(targetNode.DominantResourceShare())
  
  -	revert := t.WorkloadCq.SimulateUsageAddition(t.WorkloadInfo.Usage())
  +	restored := t.WorkloadInfo.Usage()
  +	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
  +		restored = reclaimedQuotaUsage(restored.Quota.Assigned, t.ReducedInfo.Usage().Quota.Assigned)
  +	}
  +	revert := t.WorkloadCq.SimulateUsageAddition(restored)
   	oldShare := fairsharing.TargetOldShare(targetNode.DominantResourceShare())
   	revert()
  ```
  
  </dd>
  </dl>
  </details>
  
  With the default strategies, the first strategy ignores the old share. A partial target with inflated old share can therefore pass the second strategy (`LessThanInitialShare`) when it should fail. Verification then accepts a target set that violates fair sharing. The trigger requires the alpha `PartialPreemption` gate and an annotated job.

  **Keep WorkloadPreempted set when a second-pass admission keeps a reclaim target.**
  
  A second-pass admission keeps the existing `ReclaimTargetCount` but resets `WorkloadPreempted` to False. `partialPreemptionTimedOut` then returns false for that workload, so the partial scale-down never falls back to full eviction.
  
  <details>
  <summary>Suggested direction</summary>
  <dl>
  <dd>
  
  Preserve the `WorkloadPreempted` True condition in the second-pass admission path when any `PodSetAssignments[i].ReclaimTargetCount` is retained. Alternatively, clear the retained counts in that path. Either choice keeps the two fields consistent. No safe localized diff is established, so none is included.
  
  </dd>
  </dl>
  </details>

  **Validate the partial-preemption max-reclaimed-count annotation before admission.**
  
  The Workload webhook and the Job path accept any value for `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption-max-reclaimed-count`. The scheduler treats a malformed or non-positive value as if the annotation were absent. The workload then reclaims replicas down to `minCount` in one round, which removes the intended per-round limit. Validate that the value is a positive integer in `ValidateWorkload` and in the Job webhook, which copies it to the Workload.
  
  As per path instructions, validating webhooks must reject invalid input with clear error messages.

  **Build the condition inside the retry closure.**
  
  `WithRetryOnConflict()` reruns the update closure on a refetched Workload after a conflict. `SetConditionAndUpdate` builds `condition` before that closure, so retries persist the pre-conflict `ObservedGeneration` and `LastTransitionTime`. Build the condition inside the closure so each attempt uses current values.

This PR implements the proactive batch/Job design. KEP-975 (#14589), which the description points to, now specifies reactive downscaling: evict Pods, release quota through `status.reclaimablePods`, cover Ray and Spark in Alpha, and never mutate the Job spec. The KEP's Implementation History lists this PR as the "proactive proof of concept". Please confirm with the KEP reviewers which design this PR should follow before polishing further. The inline comments mark the divergences, plus bugs that apply to either design.


  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  Quota is released before the shed Pods are gone:
  1. `RespondToPartialPreemption` lowers `spec.parallelism`.
  2. This scale-down branch immediately writes the lower count to the Workload's `spec.podSets[].count`.
  3. Admitted usage follows that count (`pkg/workload/workload.go:901-908`).
  
  So the cache frees the quota on the next reconcile, while the Job's extra Pods are still terminating. The preemptor can then be admitted onto capacity those Pods still hold. One of the KEP's Goals is "Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down". The KEP releases quota through `status.reclaimablePods` only after no Pod above the target is left. A user-driven elastic scale-down already behaves this way, but here the preemptor is admitted against exactly the quota that is released early.

```diff
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+type preemptionPlan struct {
+	target  *Target
+	partial bool
+	pending bool
+}
+
+// freedQuotaUsage returns the quota that committing this plan frees from the target ClusterQueue:
+// only the reclaimed slice for a partial preemption, or the whole workload for a whole eviction.
+// It is computed on demand so callers that don't gate on it (e.g. the classical path) pay nothing.
+func (p preemptionPlan) freedQuotaUsage() workload.Usage {
+	if p.target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		return reclaimedQuotaUsage(p.target.WorkloadInfo.Usage().Quota.Assigned, p.target.ReducedInfo.Usage().Quota.Assigned)
+	}
+	return p.target.WorkloadInfo.Usage()
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it. It leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged.
+//
+// It prefers partial preemption. For an elastic candidate, it finds the largest target count that
+// makes the preemptor fit, so it only reclaims the quota actually needed. If this candidate alone
+// cannot make the preemptor fit, it is reduced to its minimum and later candidates may add more
+// partial progress. If the preemptor still cannot fit after those candidates are considered, a
+// subsequent iterator round revisits this candidate; since it is already at its minimum, it is then
+// handled as a whole-eviction candidate.
+// With the PartialPreemption gate off, partialPreemptionInfoFor returns nil and this reduces to the
+// original whole-eviction behavior.
+func planPreemption(preemptionCtx *preemptionCtx, candidate *workload.Info, reason string, allowBorrowing, usageSimulated bool) preemptionPlan {
+	if target, timedOut := pendingPartialPreemptionTarget(preemptionCtx, candidate, reason); timedOut {
+		// Timed out: the real path falls back to whole eviction.
+		return preemptionPlan{target: &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}}
+	} else if target != nil {
+		// An in-flight partial reclaim; only the reclaimed slice will be freed.
+		return preemptionPlan{target: target, partial: true, pending: true}
+	}
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		var targetCount int32
+		var reducedInfo *workload.Info
+		if preemptionCtx.enableOnDemandPreemption {
+			// searchPartialReduction leaves the snapshot unchanged; YieldFromSnapshot applies
+			// the chosen reduction once when the target is yielded.
+			targetCount, reducedInfo, _ = searchPartialReduction(preemptionCtx, candidate, ppInfo, allowBorrowing, usageSimulated)
+		} else {
+			// On-demand preemption is disabled: drop straight to the per-round floor instead of
+			// searching for the smallest reduction that admits the preemptor. minTargetCount
+			// already folds in the per-workload maxReclaimedCount cap.

+			reducedInfo = reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, targetCount)

+				},

+func pendingPartialPreemptionTarget(preemptionCtx *preemptionCtx, candidate *workload.Info, reason string) (*Target, bool) {

+		}, false
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  With the default configuration, this fallback never runs. `timeout` has no default, 0 means disabled (`configuration_types.go:332-335`), and validation accepts 0. So a victim that never shrinks blocks the preemptor indefinitely, for example a Job whose Pods do not go away, or the non-elastic case noted on `partial_preemption.go`. The KEP defaults `partialPreemption.timeout` to 5m, requires a positive value, and starts the clock on a dedicated `PartiallyPreempted` condition.
  
  Here the clock reads `WorkloadPreempted`, which this PR sets to True on a Workload that stays admitted (`preemption.go:377`). Other readers of that condition will see a whole preemption that did not happen. One example is `GetQueueOrderTimestamp` (`pkg/workload/workload.go:1344`) for `InCohortReclaimWhileBorrowing`.

  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  Nothing here requires the Workload to be elastic. Take a plain batch/Job with `partial-preemption: "true"` and `job-min-parallelism`. It is eligible, because `MinCountsUsable` keeps its `minCount`. Then `isPartialPreemptionScaleDown` (`job_webhook.go:261`) lets `RespondToPartialPreemption` lower its parallelism while it runs. But only `workloadslicing` lowers a Workload's spec count. As far as I can trace, the Workload count therefore never drops, `HasPendingReclaimTargetCount` stays true, and quota stays charged for Pods that are gone. I traced this in code but did not run it.
  
  The same applies to other integrations. `prepareWorkload` copies the opt-in annotation for every job type (`reconciler.go:2150`), but only batch/Job implements `JobWithPartialPreemption`. Any other opted-in integration with a `minCount` gets a target that nothing acts on. Should eligibility require an elastic Workload whose integration implements the handler, for example `workloadslicing.Enabled(wl)` plus an integration check? The alternative is to have the webhook reject the annotation without `kueue.x-k8s.io/elastic-job`.


  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  For a partial target, this passes the full `WorkloadInfo` to `SimulatePodRemoval`. That removes every Pod of the victim from the scheduling simulator (`PreemptWorkload` by object key), while quota accounting keeps the reduced copy. With `SchedulerLibraryIntegration` on, a preemptor considered later in the same cycle can be placed on nodes the victim's remaining Pods still occupy. Skipping partial targets here is the safe choice: the simulation is then stricter than reality, as the comment in `SimulatePodRemoval` already accepts. The merge with main added the same pattern in `pkg/scheduler/assignment/native/native.go:123` and `:162`, which also pass the full `WorkloadInfo` of a partial target.

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  Turning the gate off breaks updates to Workloads created while it was on. An opted-in elastic Workload keeps its `minCount`, because `MinCountsUsable` returns true through `IsPartialPreemptionJob`. With `PartialPreemption` off, and `ElasticJobsViaWorkloadSlicesWithPartialReplicaScaleUp` at its default of off, this check fails. `ValidateWorkloadUpdate` calls `ValidateWorkload` on every update, and the webhook also covers `workloads/status` (line 82). So after a rollback, every write to such a Workload is rejected, including finishing it, evicting it, and releasing its quota. Could this check apply only to new or changed `minCount` values, plus a test that toggles the gate?


  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  Each round walks the whole flattened candidate list and wraps only at the end (line 184). Here is the problem:
  1. An earlier partial victim is capped by `partial-preemption-max-reclaimed-count`, so it sheds one step.
  2. The iterator then reaches a later whole-eviction candidate in the same round, for example a same-queue one.
  3. That candidate is evicted, even though one more step on the partial victim would have fit the preemptor.
  
  Fill-back can then drop the reduction and keep only the whole eviction. The KEP's "Consuming candidates across rounds" section keeps the six segments apart: in-flight candidates first, then multi-round walks per segment, and a later segment is not entered while an earlier one can still shed.

  **Synchronize the opt-in on existing Workloads.**

  **Preserve lifecycle updates for existing Jobs.**
  
  If `PartialPreemption` is enabled after a Job was created with the annotation but no `podFailurePolicy`, the webhook rejects Kueue’s suspend and unsuspend patches. The Job cannot stop or start until it is corrected or the opt-in is removed. Allow these lifecycle updates while preventing unsafe partial preemption.

```diff
+func consume(it CandidateIterator, cq *schdcache.ClusterQueueSnapshot, steps map[string]int) []string {
+	var got []string
+	for {
+		wl, _ := it.Next(false)
+		if wl == nil {
+			break
+		}
+		got = append(got, wl.Obj.Name)
+		steps[wl.Obj.Name]--
+		if steps[wl.Obj.Name] <= 0 {
+			delete(cq.Workloads, workload.Key(wl.Obj))
+		}
+	}
+	return got
+}
+
+// buildIterator lays a variable-length per-slot candidate layout into either
+// iterator, selected by cache, so both implementations can be asserted to yield
+// the same order.
+func buildIterator(cq *schdcache.ClusterQueueSnapshot, segments [][]*workload.Info, cache, partial bool, ctx *HierarchicalPreemptionCtx) CandidateIterator {
+	frs := sets.New(resources.FlavorResource{Flavor: testFlavor, Resource: corev1.ResourceCPU})
+	if !cache {
+		singleRoundGroups := numEvictedGroups
+		if !partial {
+			singleRoundGroups = len(segments)
+		}
+		var segArr [numSegments][]*candidateElem
+		for i, slot := range segments {
+			if i >= numSegments {
+				break
+			}
+			elems := make([]*candidateElem, len(slot))
+			for j, info := range slot {
+				elems[j] = &candidateElem{wl: info}
+			}
+			segArr[i] = elems
+		}
+		return &candidateIterator{
+			segments:          segArr,
+			singleRoundGroups: singleRoundGroups,
+			bandStart:         -1,
+			snapshot: &schdcache.Snapshot{
+				Manager: hierarchy.NewManagerForTest(
+					map[kueue.CohortReference]*schdcache.CohortSnapshot{},
+					map[kueue.ClusterQueueReference]*schdcache.ClusterQueueSnapshot{preemptorCQName: cq},
+				),
+			},
+			hierarchicalReclaimCtx: ctx,
+			frsNeedPreemption:      frs,
+		}
+	}
+
+	cmp := func(a, b *workload.Info) int {
+		if d := int(priority.Priority(a.Obj)) - int(priority.Priority(b.Obj)); d != 0 {
+			return d
+		}
+		return strings.Compare(a.Obj.Name, b.Obj.Name)
+	}
+	var segs [numSegments][]candidateBucket
+	for i, slot := range segments {
+		if i >= numSegments || len(slot) == 0 {
+			continue
+		}
+		var b candidateBucket
+		if partial && i >= numEvictedGroups {
+			b = &partialBucket{cq: cq, isSameQueue: true, sortedWorkloads: slot, bandStart: -1}
+		} else {
+			b = &bucket{cq: cq, isSameQueue: true, sortedWorkloads: slot}
+		}
+		segs[i] = []candidateBucket{b}
+	}
+	return &cacheCandidateIterator{
+		segments:               segs,
+		cmp:                    cmp,
+		hierarchicalReclaimCtx: ctx,
+		frsNeedPreemption:      frs,
+	}
+}
+
+// TestCandidateIteratorOrder exercises the multi-round and strict-priority band
+// logic of the non-cache iterator (and the cache iterator for parity) over the full
+// 6-slot layout. Both implementations must yield the identical order.
+func TestCandidateIteratorOrder(t *testing.T) {
+	cases := map[string]struct {
+		partial  bool
+		segments [][]*workload.Info
+		steps    map[string]int
+		want     []string
+	}{
+		"strict priority preempts low band before high band": {
+			partial: true,
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v1", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v3", 1000, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v4", 1000, true, false, false),
+			}, nil, nil},
+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 2, "v2": 2, "v3": 2, "v4": 2},
+			want:  []string{"v1", "v2", "v1", "v2", "v3", "v4", "v3", "v4"},
+		},
+		"strict priority round robins within one band": {
+			partial: true,
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v1", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v3", 0, true, false, false),
+			}, nil, nil},
+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 2, "v2": 2, "v3": 2},
+			want:  []string{"v1", "v2", "v3", "v1", "v2", "v3"},
+		},
+		"pending reclaim target is reused in the evicted pass then keeps shedding": {
+			partial: true,
+			// A pending candidate (v2) sits in both an evicted and a non-evicted slot.
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, true, true, false),
+			}, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, true, true, false),
+				candidate(t, "v1", 0, true, false, false),
+			}},
+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 1, "v2": 2},
+			want:  []string{"v2", "v2", "v1"},
+		},
+		"within a band a whole-eviction peer is taken in order, a partial peer keeps shedding": {
+			partial: true,
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v1", 0, false, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, true, false, false),
+			}, nil, nil},
+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 1, "v2": 2},
+			// Same band: v1 (whole-eviction) is yielded once at its position, v2
+			// (partial) keeps the round alive and is shed across rounds.
+			want: []string{"v1", "v2", "v2"},
+		},
+		"a partial peer rolls the band over past a whole-eviction peer": {
+			partial: true,
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "v1", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "v2", 0, false, false, false),
+			}, nil, nil},
+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 2, "v2": 1},
+			// Same band: v2 (whole-eviction) is taken once, v1 (partial) is shed
+			// again in the next round because it kept the band alive.
+			want: []string{"v1", "v2", "v1"},
+		},
+		"lower band is fully shed (shrink then evict) before the higher band is touched": {
+			partial: true,
+			segments: [][]*workload.Info{nil, nil, nil, {
+				candidate(t, "low", 0, true, false, false),
+				candidate(t, "high", 1000, true, false, false),
+			}, nil, nil},
+			steps: map[string]int{"low": 2, "high": 1},
+			// Strict priority: the low band shrinks and whole-evicts to exhaustion

+			want: []string{"low", "low", "high"},

+			steps: map[string]int{"v1": 1, "v2": 1, "v3": 1},
+			want:  []string{"v1", "v2", "v3"},
+		},
```

  **Advance to the next priority band.**
  
  When `PartialPreemption` is enabled, a band containing only whole-eviction candidates ends the segment even if later bands contain eligible victims. The preemptor can return no targets and leave a workload pending despite sufficient reclaimable quota. Advance to the next band in both iterator implementations.

  **Use recency for jobs that cannot shrink.**
  
  When an older partial-preemption job is at its floor, it can be wholly evicted before a newer equal-priority job. Both require whole eviction, so this can discard more completed work. Apply the partial-preemption preference only when the candidate can shrink.

  **Bound the partial-preemption search**
  
  When a large non-TAS victim is eligible but a TAS PodSet remains blocked, the search reduces the victim one replica at a time. It reruns topology placement after quota fits. Thousands of checks can delay admission across queues. Bound the search while preserving priority ordering.

```diff
@@ -510,27 +576,231 @@ func (p *Preemptor) getTargets(ctx context.Context, strategies iter.Seq[Preempti
 	return nil
 }
 
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it; it leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged. For an elastic candidate it shrinks the reclaimable PodSet by one replica,
+// bounded below by preemptionMinCount; otherwise, and with the gate off, it is a whole eviction.
+func planPreemption(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		reducedInfo := reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, ppInfo.targetCount)
+		return &Target{
+			WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+			Reason:       reason,
+			PartialPreemption: &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{
+				PodSet:                ppInfo.podSet,
+				PreemptionTargetCount: ppInfo.targetCount,
+			},
+			ReducedInfo: reducedInfo,
+		}
+	}
+	// Whole eviction.
+	return &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}
+}
+
+// applyPreemption simulates preempting the target in the snapshot: for a partial target it replaces
+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+}
+
+// revertPreemption is the inverse of applyPreemption: it removes the reduced copy of a partial target
+// (if any) and adds the full workload back, restoring the snapshot to its pre-preemption state.
+func revertPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+	snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+}

+type partialPreemptionInfo struct {
+	podSet kueue.PodSetReference
+	// targetCount is this round's reclaim target: used-1 bounded below by preemptionMinCount, or a
+	// still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount reused directly.
+	targetCount int32
+}
+
+// partialPreemptionInfoFor returns this round's reclaim target for candidate, or nil to fall back to
+// whole eviction. A still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount is reused directly; otherwise the
+// target is used-1 (the per-round cap) bounded below by preemptionMinCount. Eligible only when
+// exactly one PodSet is above its floor and has no topology assignment.
+func partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate *workload.Info) *partialPreemptionInfo {
+	// An already-evicted victim has a whole eviction in flight; its full quota is being
+	// reclaimed. Re-planning it as a scale-down would under-count the freed quota and push
+	// the preemptor to evict another victim, causing cross-cycle oscillation.
+	if !candidate.IsPartialPreemptionJob || workloadevict.IsEvicted(candidate.Obj) {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	floors := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32, len(candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets))
+	for i := range candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets {
+		ps := &candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets[i]
+		if ps.PreemptionMinCount != nil {
+			floors[ps.Name] = *ps.PreemptionMinCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(floors) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	var info *partialPreemptionInfo
+	for _, psr := range candidate.TotalRequests {
+		minCount, ok := floors[psr.Name]
+		if !ok {
+			continue
+		}
+		used := psr.Count
+		if used <= minCount {
+			// Already at or below its floor; this PodSet cannot shrink further.
+			continue
+		}
+		// TODO: TAS victims are not supported yet. A shrinkable PodSet with a topology
+		// assignment forces whole eviction.
+		if psr.TopologyRequest != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// At most one PodSet may shrink per candidate today; a second one above its floor
+		// falls back to whole eviction.
+		if info != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// A pending reclaim target still draining: reuse it so the candidate stays preemptible while
+		// its Pods terminate.
+		if target, ok := workload.PreemptionTargetCount(candidate.Obj, psr.Name); ok && used > target {
+			info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+				podSet:      psr.Name,
+				targetCount: target,
+			}
+			continue
+		}
+		// Reclaim one replica this round (target used-1, bounded by preemptionMinCount); later rounds
+		// deepen it.
+		reclaimable := min(used-minCount, maxReclaimedReplicasPerRound)
+		info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+			podSet:      psr.Name,
+			targetCount: used - reclaimable,
+		}
+	}
+	return info
+}
+

-		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)

 }

+//
+// Without partial preemption the targets are appended in a single pass, so the last target is the
+// one that achieved the fit and is always needed: reverting it never fits, so it is left untouched
+// and is not a drop candidate. With partial preemption a later-round whole eviction can replace an
+// earlier round's reduced target in place (see addOrReplaceTarget), so the fit-achieving entry is
+// not necessarily the last one in the slice — a stale over-reclaimed reduction can sit at the tail.
+// In that case the last target is reconsidered too: reverting and re-checking the fit drops it only
+// when it was genuinely unneeded.

+func fillbackPartialPreemption(ctx context.Context, preemptionCtx *preemptionCtx, t *Target, allowBorrowing bool) {
+	if !t.WorkloadInfo.IsPartialPreemptionJob {
+		return
+	}
+	var podSet kueue.PodSetReference
+	lo, high := int32(0), int32(-1)
+	for i := range t.WorkloadInfo.Obj.Spec.PodSets {
+		ps := &t.WorkloadInfo.Obj.Spec.PodSets[i]
+		if ps.PreemptionMinCount == nil {
+			continue
+		}
+		podSet, lo = ps.Name, *ps.PreemptionMinCount
+		if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			for j := range t.ReducedInfo.TotalRequests {
+				if t.ReducedInfo.TotalRequests[j].Name == podSet {
+					lo = t.ReducedInfo.TotalRequests[j].Count + 1
+					break
+				}
+			}
+		}
+		break
+	}
+	if podSet == "" {
+		return
+	}
+	for i := range t.WorkloadInfo.TotalRequests {
+		if t.WorkloadInfo.TotalRequests[i].Name == podSet {
+			high = t.WorkloadInfo.TotalRequests[i].Count - 1
+			if pending, has := workload.PreemptionTargetCount(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj, podSet); has && pending < high {
+				high = pending
+			}
+			break
+		}
+	}
+
+	if lo > high {
+		return
+	}
+	cur := t.WorkloadInfo
+	fitsAt := func(c int32) bool {
+		preemptionCtx.snapshot.RemoveWorkload(cur)
+		cur = reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(t.WorkloadInfo, podSet, c)
+		preemptionCtx.snapshot.AddWorkload(cur)
+		return workloadFits(ctx, preemptionCtx, allowBorrowing)
+	}
+	k := sort.Search(int(high-lo+1), func(k int) bool { return !fitsAt(lo + int32(k)) })
+	best := lo + int32(k) - 1
+	// Restore the full victim; the caller applies the chosen plan.
+	preemptionCtx.snapshot.RemoveWorkload(cur)
+	preemptionCtx.snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if best < lo {
+		return
+	}
+	t.ReducedInfo = reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(t.WorkloadInfo, podSet, best)
+	t.PartialPreemption = &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{PodSet: podSet, PreemptionTargetCount: best}
```

  **Keep already-evicted targets as whole evictions.**
  
  When an evicted workload still holds quota, fill-back can convert it to a partial target. This patches a partial-preemption request onto a workload already stopping and accounts for less than its full pending quota release. Skip fill-back for evicted workloads.

```diff
 }
+
+// reducedInfoForPartialPreemption returns a shallow copy of info with the named PodSet's usage scaled
+// down to targetCount. The original is left untouched so the snapshot can restore it unchanged.
+func reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(info *workload.Info, podSetName kueue.PodSetReference, targetCount int32) *workload.Info {
+	reduced := *info
+	reduced.TotalRequests = make([]workload.PodSetResources, len(info.TotalRequests))
+	for idx := range info.TotalRequests {
+		psr := info.TotalRequests[idx]
+		if psr.Name == podSetName {
+			if targetCount >= 0 && targetCount < psr.Count {
+				psr = *psr.ScaledTo(targetCount)
+			}
+		}
+		reduced.TotalRequests[idx] = psr
+	}
+	return &reduced
+}
+
+// setPreemptionTargetCount records count as the named PodSet's requested partial-preemption target,
+// reporting whether the value changed.
+func setPreemptionTargetCount(wl *kueue.Workload, podSetName kueue.PodSetReference, count int32) bool {
+	if wl.Status.Admission == nil {
+		return false
+	}
+	for i := range wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments {
+		psa := &wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[i]
+		if psa.Name == podSetName {
+			if ptr.Deref(psa.PreemptionTargetCount, -1) == count {
+				return false
+			}
+			psa.PreemptionTargetCount = new(count)
+			return true
+		}
+	}
+	return false
+}
```

  **Prevent stale plans from raising an in-flight target.**
  
  If a new scheduling cycle uses a stale victim, its higher target can overwrite a lower target already requested. The job can then stop shedding too early, delaying admission or causing further preemptions. Check the resource version and retry against the current target before updating it.

  ```
  Update setPreemptionTargetCount and its persistence path so a target update is
  conditional on the workload’s current resource version; on conflict, reload the
  workload and recompute against the current target before retrying. Ensure a
  ```

  **Preserve reduced usage during overlap recomputation.**
  
  When an earlier entry partially preempts a victim, this simulation removes its full usage. A later entry can select too few other victims, fail the subsequent fit check, and be skipped despite available victims. Simulate the retained usage instead.
  
  <!-- ai-agent-prompt:start -->
  
  <details>
  <summary><picture><source media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)" srcset="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/bot-dark.svg"><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/bot.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

  ```
  retained usage after partial preemption, rather than removing its full usage
  ```

  **Set the example Job's completion mode to Indexed.**
  
  When `PartialPreemption` is enabled, the webhook rejects this example because its completion mode defaults to `NonIndexed`. Set it to `Indexed` so users can create the Job.
  
  <details>
  <summary>Suggested fix</summary>
  <dl>
  <dd>
  <br>
  
  ```diff
   spec:
  +  completionMode: Indexed
     parallelism: 10
  ```
  
  </dd>
  </dl>
  </details>

```diff
+// releaseReclaimedQuota lowers each partial-preemption PodSet's admitted count toward its reclaim
+// target as its ungated Pods drain, scaling resourceUsage with it.
+func (r *elasticJobUngater) releaseReclaimedQuota(ctx context.Context, wl *kueue.Workload, ungatedPerPodSet map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32) error {
+	if !apimeta.IsStatusConditionTrue(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted) {
+		return nil
+	}
+	log := ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx)
+	wlCopy := wl.DeepCopy()
+	if err := patching.PatchAdmissionStatus(ctx, r.client, wlCopy, r.clock, func(wl *kueue.Workload) (bool, error) {
+		// Recompute from scratch: the closure may run again on a patch conflict.
+		updated := false
+		for i := range wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments {
+			psa := &wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[i]
+			if psa.PreemptionTargetCount == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			target := *psa.PreemptionTargetCount
+			admitCount := ptr.Deref(psa.Count, target)
+			newCount := max(min(admitCount, ungatedPerPodSet[psa.Name]), target)
+			if newCount >= admitCount {
+				continue
+			}
+			// Scale resourceUsage in proportion to the count so count and usage stay consistent.
+			req := resources.NewRequestsFromResourceList(psa.ResourceUsage)
+			req.Divide(int64(admitCount))
+			req.Mul(int64(newCount))
+			psa.ResourceUsage = req.ToResourceList(resources.NewResourceFormatter())
+			psa.Count = new(newCount)
+			updated = true
+		}
+		return updated, nil
+	}, patching.WithLooseOnApply()); err != nil {
```

  **Reject stale quota-release updates.**
  
  When the scheduler lowers a reclaim target during reconciliation, this loose apply can restore the older target. Reclaim can then stop too early, delaying the preemptor. Use a resourceVersion precondition so reconciliation retries on conflict.

```diff
@@ -484,6 +494,24 @@ func (j *Job) podSetMinCount() *int32 {

+// podSetPreemptionMinCount returns the partial-preemption scale-down floor read from the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption-min-count annotation, or nil when the feature gate is off
+// or the annotation is absent or malformed.
+func (j *Job) podSetPreemptionMinCount() *int32 {
+	if !features.Enabled(features.PartialPreemption) {
+		return nil
+	}
+	strVal, found := j.GetAnnotations()[PartialPreemptionMinCountAnnotation]
+	if !found {
+		return nil
+	}
+	minCount, err := strconv.ParseInt(strVal, 10, 32)
+	if err != nil || minCount < 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
```

  **Validate the partial-preemption floor annotation.**
  
  When an opted-in Job has a malformed, negative, or above-count floor, the webhook accepts it. The scheduler can then evict the whole Workload instead of shrinking it, without reporting the invalid floor. Reject invalid values when the annotation is created or changed.

```diff
+// validatePartialPreemptionCreate validates a Job that opts into partial preemption via the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption="true" annotation. Partial preemption reclaims quota
+// reactively: Kueue evicts the Job's excess Pods and relies on the Job controller to recreate
+// them gated, rather than patching the Job. For batch/Job that is only safe when an evicted Pod
+// neither counts toward backoffLimit nor, on a clean SIGTERM exit, counts as a completion, so the
+// Job must use Indexed completion, restartPolicy Never, and a podFailurePolicy rule that ignores
+// the DisruptionTarget condition. The checks apply only when the PartialPreemption gate is on.
```

  **Prevent interrupted indexes from reporting success.**
  
  If an evicted container exits zero before completing its work, Kubernetes counts its index as succeeded. The Job and Kueue Workload can then finish with missing results. The `DisruptionTarget` Ignore rule only handles failed Pods. Require an eviction-safe completion mechanism before enabling this path.

  **Defer equal-count probes during reclaim.**
  
  If partial preemption lowers the admitted count while the target is pending, the equal-count branch creates a scale-up probe before this guard runs. A pending probe then prevents the Job reconciler from retrying victim evictions, which can delay preemption. Apply the pending-target check to this branch too.


  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  With the gate on, this lets any admitted Workload rewrite `resourceUsage`, as long as `count` does not go up. That includes an unchanged count. Nothing checks that the Workload is elastic, opted in, or has a target, or that the usage stays in proportion to the count.
  
  For example, a status update on an ordinary 4-Pod Workload can keep count 4 and set CPU usage to 0. It passes, and the cache then charges nothing for four running Pods. The `preemptionTargetCount` copy above also has no bounds, as in my comment on the old head.
  
  The KEP's Validation section lists the checks:
  - the target stays within [floor, count) and is never raised;
  - `count` decreases only for an elastic Workload with a target;
  - `resourceUsage` stays in proportion to the count.


  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  This guard runs too late for the reactive release:
  1. The ungater lowers `psa.Count`, but the Job and Workload spec counts stay the same.
  2. So the equal-count branch above (lines 99-104) sees spec > admitted and treats it as partial admission.
  3. It returns a scale-up probe before execution reaches this check.
  
  As far as I can trace, the new slice then becomes the selected Workload. It is not admitted, so the reconciler returns at `jobframework/reconciler.go:668`, before `reconcilePartialPreemption` at line 678. The remaining over-target Pods are then no longer evicted, for example when a PDB blocked some evictions. I traced this but did not run it.
  
  The KEP's Growing back section says the scheduler does not admit a scale-up slice while Pods above a target are still running. Could the pending-target check move ahead of the equal-count branch? CodeRabbit flagged the same branch at `manager.go:130`.

```diff
@@ -510,27 +576,231 @@ func (p *Preemptor) getTargets(ctx context.Context, strategies iter.Seq[Preempti
 	return nil
 }
 
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it; it leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged. For an elastic candidate it shrinks the reclaimable PodSet by one replica,
+// bounded below by preemptionMinCount; otherwise, and with the gate off, it is a whole eviction.
+func planPreemption(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		reducedInfo := reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, ppInfo.targetCount)
+		return &Target{
+			WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+			Reason:       reason,
+			PartialPreemption: &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{
+				PodSet:                ppInfo.podSet,
+				PreemptionTargetCount: ppInfo.targetCount,
+			},
+			ReducedInfo: reducedInfo,
+		}
+	}
+	// Whole eviction.
+	return &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}
+}
+
+// applyPreemption simulates preempting the target in the snapshot: for a partial target it replaces
+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+}
+
+// revertPreemption is the inverse of applyPreemption: it removes the reduced copy of a partial target
+// (if any) and adds the full workload back, restoring the snapshot to its pre-preemption state.
+func revertPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+	snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+}
+
+type partialPreemptionInfo struct {
+	podSet kueue.PodSetReference
+	// targetCount is this round's reclaim target: used-1 bounded below by preemptionMinCount, or a
+	// still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount reused directly.
+	targetCount int32
+}
+
+// partialPreemptionInfoFor returns this round's reclaim target for candidate, or nil to fall back to
+// whole eviction. A still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount is reused directly; otherwise the
+// target is used-1 (the per-round cap) bounded below by preemptionMinCount. Eligible only when
+// exactly one PodSet is above its floor and has no topology assignment.
+func partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate *workload.Info) *partialPreemptionInfo {
+	// An already-evicted victim has a whole eviction in flight; its full quota is being
+	// reclaimed. Re-planning it as a scale-down would under-count the freed quota and push
+	// the preemptor to evict another victim, causing cross-cycle oscillation.
+	if !candidate.IsPartialPreemptionJob || workloadevict.IsEvicted(candidate.Obj) {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	floors := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32, len(candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets))
+	for i := range candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets {
+		ps := &candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets[i]
+		if ps.PreemptionMinCount != nil {
+			floors[ps.Name] = *ps.PreemptionMinCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(floors) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	var info *partialPreemptionInfo
+	for _, psr := range candidate.TotalRequests {
+		minCount, ok := floors[psr.Name]
+		if !ok {
+			continue
+		}
+		used := psr.Count
+		if used <= minCount {
+			// Already at or below its floor; this PodSet cannot shrink further.
+			continue
+		}
+		// TODO: TAS victims are not supported yet. A shrinkable PodSet with a topology
+		// assignment forces whole eviction.
+		if psr.TopologyRequest != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// At most one PodSet may shrink per candidate today; a second one above its floor
+		// falls back to whole eviction.
+		if info != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// A pending reclaim target still draining: reuse it so the candidate stays preemptible while
+		// its Pods terminate.
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  Fill-back can turn any needed opted-in target into a partial one, but it checks only `IsPartialPreemptionJob`. It skips the rules in `partialPreemptionInfoFor` (lines 651-690):
  - the victim must not be evicted already;
  - the shrinkable PodSet must have no topology request;
  - at most one PodSet may have a floor (here the first PodSet with a floor is taken).
  
  So a TAS victim that was correctly planned as a whole eviction can come out of fill-back with a target and a lowered count, while its topology assignment stays unchanged.
  
  `fillBackWorkloads` also runs for Fair Sharing, so a Fair Sharing target can become partial even though the KEP defers Fair Sharing shrinking to Beta. `verifyFairSharingTarget` then adds the full usage back on top of the reduced victim (`fair_strategy.go:216`). Reusing the same eligibility check here would cover all of these. So would filling back only targets that already carry `PartialPreemption`.

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **Claude Opus 5.5 responding on behalf of vladikkuzn**
  
  sweinberg's case still fails at this head. `currentWorkload` only skips victims that were already removed, and here it never gets the chance:
  1. With no partial candidate in the band, `roundHasRemainingCandidate` stays false. Only lines 299-301 set it.
  2. The cursor reaches the priority-5 victim, and the `inCurrentBand` check breaks the loop.
  3. Line 304 then returns `ok=false`, which ends the segment.
  
  So the priority-5 victim is never yielded and `getTargets` returns no targets. With the gate off, both victims are preempted. `partialBucket.nextEligible` has the same exit (`cache_candidate_generator.go:241`).
  
  A fix for both: start a new round when the band boundary is reached, and stop only when a full round yields nothing.
  
  A related point: the band compares `priority.Priority`, the base priority (line 225), but the list is sorted by `EffectivePriority` (`ordering.go:76`). With `PriorityBoost` on, a band can end early on a candidate whose effective priority belongs in it.

```diff
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it; it leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged. For an elastic candidate it shrinks the reclaimable PodSet by one replica,
+// bounded below by preemptionMinCount; otherwise, and with the gate off, it is a whole eviction.
+func planPreemption(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		reducedInfo := reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, ppInfo.targetCount)
+		return &Target{
+			WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+			Reason:       reason,
+			PartialPreemption: &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{
+				PodSet:                ppInfo.podSet,
+				PreemptionTargetCount: ppInfo.targetCount,
+			},
+			ReducedInfo: reducedInfo,

+	}

+	return &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}

+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {

+	}
+}
+
+// revertPreemption is the inverse of applyPreemption: it removes the reduced copy of a partial target
+// (if any) and adds the full workload back, restoring the snapshot to its pre-preemption state.
+func revertPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {

+	}

+}
+
+type partialPreemptionInfo struct {

+	// still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount reused directly.

+// partialPreemptionInfoFor returns this round's reclaim target for candidate, or nil to fall back to
+// whole eviction. A still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount is reused directly; otherwise the
+// target is used-1 (the per-round cap) bounded below by preemptionMinCount. Eligible only when
+// exactly one PodSet is above its floor and has no topology assignment.
+func partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate *workload.Info) *partialPreemptionInfo {
+	// An already-evicted victim has a whole eviction in flight; its full quota is being
+	// reclaimed. Re-planning it as a scale-down would under-count the freed quota and push
+	// the preemptor to evict another victim, causing cross-cycle oscillation.
+	if !candidate.IsPartialPreemptionJob || workloadevict.IsEvicted(candidate.Obj) {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	floors := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32, len(candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets))
+	for i := range candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets {
+		ps := &candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets[i]
+		if ps.PreemptionMinCount != nil {
+			floors[ps.Name] = *ps.PreemptionMinCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(floors) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	var info *partialPreemptionInfo
+	for _, psr := range candidate.TotalRequests {
+		minCount, ok := floors[psr.Name]
+		if !ok {
+			continue
+		}
+		used := psr.Count
+		if used <= minCount {
+			// Already at or below its floor; this PodSet cannot shrink further.
+			continue

+		// assignment forces whole eviction.
+		if psr.TopologyRequest != nil {

+		}
+		// At most one PodSet may shrink per candidate today; a second one above its floor
+		// falls back to whole eviction.

+			return nil
+		}

+		// its Pods terminate.
+		if target, ok := workload.PreemptionTargetCount(candidate.Obj, psr.Name); ok && used > target {
+			info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+				podSet:      psr.Name,
+				targetCount: target,
+			}
+			continue
+		}

+		// deepen it.
+		reclaimable := min(used-minCount, maxReclaimedReplicasPerRound)
+		info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+			podSet:      psr.Name,
+			targetCount: used - reclaimable,

```

  This is intentional. Since preemptedWorkloads cannot currently distinguish whether a workload is subject to a "partial preempt," the RecomputeAssignmentUponPreemptionTargetsOverlap is not supported in the alpha version. I aim to implement this in beta. For now, we ensure that RecomputeAssignmentUponPreemptionTargetsOverlap and PartialPreempt are not both set to true simultaneously.

**Add cases for the count and `resourceUsage` convergence branch, and drop the zero-value sentinel.**

`validateAdmissionUpdate` has a new branch that accepts a lower `count` and `resourceUsage` and rejects a higher `count`. The table only covers `preemptionTargetCount`. Add cases for these paths:

- A count decrease with scaled `resourceUsage` is accepted when the gate is on.
- A count increase is rejected when the gate is on.
- A count decrease is rejected when the gate is off.

The `target == 0` to `1` remap makes `target: 0` unreachable and hides the intent. Set `target` explicitly in each case.

  **Prevent replay of unchanged partial candidates.**
  
  A caller that drains `CandidateIterator.Next` without changing the snapshot can receive the same partial candidate indefinitely. Skip unchanged candidates on replay, while preserving further rounds when the snapshot contains a reduced candidate.
  
  <!-- ai-agent-prompt:start -->

  ```
  Review comment at @pkg/scheduler/preemption/classical/candidate_generator.go around lines 298 - 306:
  Update replay handling in CandidateIterator.Next so draining it against an unchanged snapshot skips candidates already returned instead of yielding the same partial candidate indefinitely. Preserve further rounds when the snapshot has changed to contain a reduced candidate; use the iterator’s existing candidate and snapshot state to distinguish that case.
  
  After applying the fix, consider running `coderabbit review --agent` for local review. Visit https://docs.coderabbit.ai/cli?utm_source=ghpr
  ```

```diff
+func (c *candidateIterator) nextSingleRound(seg []*candidateElem, borrow bool) (*workload.Info, string, bool) {
+	for c.cursor < len(seg) {
+		candidate := seg[c.cursor]
+		c.cursor++
+		current := c.currentWorkload(candidate)
+		if current == nil || !c.candidateIsValid(current, candidate, borrow) {
+			continue

+	}

+// nextMultiRound returns the next candidate from a multi-round slot, or ok=false
+// when the slot is drained. A round walks from the top of the slot until it leaves
+// the current priority band, yielding every valid candidate in that band. If the

+			}
```
