```diff
+// validatePartialPreemptionCreate validates a Job that opts into partial preemption via the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption="true" annotation. Partial preemption reclaims quota
+// reactively: Kueue evicts the Job's excess Pods and relies on the Job controller to recreate
+// them gated, rather than patching the Job. For batch/Job that is only safe when an evicted Pod
+// neither counts toward backoffLimit nor, on a clean SIGTERM exit, counts as a completion, so the
+// Job must use Indexed completion, restartPolicy Never, and a podFailurePolicy rule that ignores
+// the DisruptionTarget condition. The checks apply only when the PartialPreemption gate is on.
+func validatePartialPreemptionCreate(job *Job) field.ErrorList {

+				batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore, corev1.DisruptionTarget)))
+	}
+	return allErrs
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  Check the effective first matching rule, not just whether any Ignore rule exists. A Job with `FailJob` for `DisruptionTarget=True` followed by an `Ignore` rule for the same condition passes `ValidateCreate`, but Kubernetes applies the first match, so a reclaimed failed Pod can fail the whole Job. Please add a competing-rules test.

```diff
+// reconcilePartialPreemption evicts the over-target Pods of an admitted elastic job whose Workload
+// carries a partial-preemption reclaim target, so each PodSet drains to its target. The integration
+// chooses which Pods leave (see JobWithPartialPreemption); without the hook nothing is evicted.
+// Eviction goes through the Eviction API so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored.
+func (r *JobReconciler) reconcilePartialPreemption(ctx context.Context, job GenericJob, wl *kueue.Workload) error {
+	if wl == nil || wl.Status.Admission == nil ||
+		!features.Enabled(features.PartialPreemption) ||
+		!workload.HasPendingPreemptionTargetCount(wl) {
+		return nil
+	}
+	targets := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32)
+	for i := range wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments {
+		psa := &wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[i]
+		if psa.PreemptionTargetCount != nil {
+			targets[psa.Name] = *psa.PreemptionTargetCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(targets) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+	pods, err := r.reclaimCandidates(ctx, job, wl, targets)
+	if err != nil || len(pods) == 0 {
+		return err
+	}
+	log := ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx)
+	return parallelize.Until(ctx, len(pods), func(i int) error {
+		return evictPodForReclaim(ctx, r.client, log, pods[i])
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  Account for the stored preemption target in Job PodsReady while reclaim is in flight. `Job.PodsReady` uses min(spec, admitted), but terminating Pods leave Kubernetes active ready counts before the ungater releases their old admission. A previously ready Job can enter WaitForRecovery during this drain and be wholly evicted if recoveryTimeout is shorter than termination grace. Include the target in the readiness denominator.

```diff
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it; it leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged. For an elastic candidate it shrinks the reclaimable PodSet by one replica,
+// bounded below by preemptionMinCount; otherwise, and with the gate off, it is a whole eviction.
+func planPreemption(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		reducedInfo := reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, ppInfo.targetCount)
+		return &Target{
+			WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+			Reason:       reason,
+			PartialPreemption: &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{
+				PodSet:                ppInfo.podSet,
+				PreemptionTargetCount: ppInfo.targetCount,
+			},
+			ReducedInfo: reducedInfo,
+		}
+	}
+	// Whole eviction.
+	return &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}
+}
+
+// applyPreemption simulates preempting the target in the snapshot: for a partial target it replaces
+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+}
+
+// revertPreemption is the inverse of applyPreemption: it removes the reduced copy of a partial target
+// (if any) and adds the full workload back, restoring the snapshot to its pre-preemption state.
+func revertPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+	snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+}
+

+}

+func partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate *workload.Info) *partialPreemptionInfo {
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  Please reconcile this selection order with the current [KEP](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14589) before landing. It describes shrinking candidates directly to their floors before whole eviction; this replays `used-1` within priority bands. For a lower-priority count 10/floor 8 and higher-priority count 10/floor 2, an incoming request for 4 can cause whole eviction of the lower Job before shrinking the higher one, while the proposal retains both. The KEP is still open; keep fillback when reconciling the approaches.

  The overlap-gate reply addresses a different path. Shared fillback can turn whole Fair Sharing and ConfigurablePreemptionReason victims into partial targets. For Fair Sharing, the verifier adds full usage on top of retained reduced usage, overstating the old share. Please keep these targets whole for Alpha. Conversion and accounting are supported by code inspection; a complete cross-queue loop was not reproduced.

  The old stuck-cap symptom is removed. With the gate off, however, cap, release and clearing depend on the gate, so an existing target can be abandoned and its condition remain stale. The current KEP calls for draining stored targets after rollback. Please honor them through cap, release and clearing, and allow only the corresponding narrow release validation after gate-off.

```diff
+
+// reconcilePartialPreemption evicts the over-target Pods of an admitted elastic job whose Workload
+// carries a partial-preemption reclaim target, so each PodSet drains to its target. The integration
+// chooses which Pods leave (see JobWithPartialPreemption); without the hook nothing is evicted.
+// Eviction goes through the Eviction API so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored.
+func (r *JobReconciler) reconcilePartialPreemption(ctx context.Context, job GenericJob, wl *kueue.Workload) error {
+	if wl == nil || wl.Status.Admission == nil ||
+		!features.Enabled(features.PartialPreemption) ||
+		!workload.HasPendingPreemptionTargetCount(wl) {
+		return nil
+	}
+	targets := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32)
+	for i := range wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments {
+		psa := &wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[i]
+		if psa.PartialPreemption != nil && psa.PartialPreemption.TargetCount != nil {
+			targets[psa.Name] = *psa.PartialPreemption.TargetCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(targets) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+	pods, err := r.reclaimCandidates(ctx, job, wl, targets)
+	if err != nil || len(pods) == 0 {
+		return err
+	}
+	return parallelize.Until(ctx, len(pods), func(i int) error {
+		return evictPodForReclaim(ctx, r.client, ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx), pods[i])
+	})
+}
+
+// reclaimCandidates returns the Pods the integration chooses to evict. Only integrations that
+// implement JobWithPartialPreemption reclaim Pods; others return none.
+func (r *JobReconciler) reclaimCandidates(ctx context.Context, job GenericJob, wl *kueue.Workload, targets map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32) ([]*corev1.Pod, error) {
+	ppJob, ok := job.(JobWithPartialPreemption)
+	if !ok {
+		return nil, nil
+	}
+	return ppJob.PodsToReclaim(ctx, r.client, wl, targets)
+}
+
+// evictPodForReclaim evicts one Pod through the Eviction API.
+func evictPodForReclaim(ctx context.Context, c client.Client, log logr.Logger, pod *corev1.Pod) error {
+	// TODO: Derive the grace period from the Kueue partial preemption timeout once it is configurable.
+	eviction := &policyv1.Eviction{
+		Name:      pod.Name,
+		Namespace: pod.Namespace,
+		DeleteOptions: &metav1.DeleteOptions{
+			GracePeriodSeconds: ptr.To[int64](30),
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  Omit `GracePeriodSeconds` so the Pod’s configured termination grace is honored. For a scheduled, nonterminal Pod configured with 300 seconds, this explicit 30-second delete option overrides that value and can interrupt checkpointing or shutdown. The current [KEP](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14589) promises the usual Pod grace period; the TODO does not establish an exception.

```diff
+// mintPod simulates a completion index; draining removes the highest indexes first.
+func mintPod(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix, workloadName string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, index int32) {
+	ginkgo.GinkgoHelper()
+	pod := testingpod.MakePod(fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", podNamePrefix, index), namespace).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Label(pkgconstants.PodSetLabel, string(podSet)).
+		Label(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Gate(kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate).
+		Obj()
+	behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, pod)
+}
+
+func expectPodCounts(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, wantUngated, wantGated int) {

+		pods := &corev1.PodList{}

+			client.MatchingLabels{pkgconstants.PodSetLabel: string(podSet)})).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		ungated, gated := 0, 0
+		for i := range pods.Items {
+			if !strings.HasPrefix(pods.Items[i].Name, podNamePrefix+"-") {
+				continue
+			}
+			if utilpod.HasGate(&pods.Items[i], kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate) {
+				gated++
+			} else {
+				ungated++
+			}
+		}
+		g.Expect(ungated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantUngated))
+		g.Expect(gated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantGated))
+	}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+}
+
+// This exercises the full reactive partial-preemption loop for batch/Job, end to end:
+//
+//	request  - the scheduler sets PreemptionTargetCount when the preemptor arrives,
+//	drain    - the Job reconciler evicts the victim's excess Pods down to the target,
+//	release  - the ungater lowers the admitted count and flips PartiallyPreempted to QuotaReleased,
+//	admit    - the freed quota admits the preemptor,
+//	re-grow  - once the preemptor leaves, the victim scales back up to its original parallelism.

+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.PartialPreemption, true)
+		fwk.StartManager(ctx, cfg, managerAndControllersSetup(false, true, nil))
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.AfterAll(func() {

+	})
+

+
+		rf = utiltestingapi.MakeResourceFlavor("default").Obj()

+
+		cq = utiltestingapi.MakeClusterQueue("job-partial-preemption-cq").
+			ResourceGroup(*utiltestingapi.MakeFlavorQuotas(rf.Name).Resource(corev1.ResourceCPU, "4").Obj()).
+			Preemption(kueue.ClusterQueuePreemption{

+			}).
+			Obj()
```

  @mimowo I’ve refactored it following your suggestion by extracting a plan layer between `strategy` and `iterator`, with `fullPlan` and `partialPlan` available. This decouples the partial logic from specific preemption algorithms.
  But enabling configurable preemption / fair preemption together with partial preemption is not supported for now, mainly due to incompatible interfaces. Besides, in fair preemption scenarios, the calls to YieldFromSnapshot and t.Apply(snapshot) are still tightly coupled within the iterator iteration flow and require further rework. I propose addressing this in a separate PR.

```diff
+
+// releaseReclaimedQuota lowers each partial-preemption PodSet's admitted count toward its reclaim
+// target as its ungated Pods drain, scaling resourceUsage with it.
+func (r *elasticJobUngater) releaseReclaimedQuota(ctx context.Context, wl *kueue.Workload, ungatedPerPodSet map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32) error {
+	if !apimeta.IsStatusConditionTrue(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted) {
+		return nil
+	}
+	log := ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx)
+	wlCopy := wl.DeepCopy()
+	if err := patching.PatchAdmissionStatus(ctx, r.client, wlCopy, r.clock, func(wl *kueue.Workload) (bool, error) {
+		// Recompute from scratch: the closure may run again on a patch conflict.
+		updated := false
+		for i := range wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments {
+			psa := &wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[i]
+			if psa.PreemptionTargetCount == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			target := *psa.PreemptionTargetCount
+			admitCount := ptr.Deref(psa.Count, target)
+			newCount := max(min(admitCount, ungatedPerPodSet[psa.Name]), target)
+			if newCount >= admitCount {
+				continue
+			}
+			// Scale resourceUsage in proportion to the count so count and usage stay consistent.
+			req := resources.NewRequestsFromResourceList(psa.ResourceUsage)
+			req.Divide(int64(admitCount))
+			req.Mul(int64(newCount))
+			psa.ResourceUsage = req.ToResourceList(resources.NewResourceFormatter())
+			psa.Count = new(newCount)
+			updated = true
+		}
+		return updated, nil
+	}, patching.WithLooseOnApply()); err != nil {
```

  Done: removed WithLooseOnApply to keep resourceVersion precondition; stale writes fail with Conflict and requeue for fresh reconcile.

```diff
+// validatePartialPreemptionCreate validates a Job that opts into partial preemption via the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption="true" annotation. Partial preemption reclaims quota
+// reactively: Kueue evicts the Job's excess Pods and relies on the Job controller to recreate
+// them gated, rather than patching the Job. For batch/Job that is only safe when an evicted Pod
+// neither counts toward backoffLimit nor, on a clean SIGTERM exit, counts as a completion, so the
+// Job must use Indexed completion, restartPolicy Never, and a podFailurePolicy rule that ignores
+// the DisruptionTarget condition. The checks apply only when the PartialPreemption gate is on.
```

```diff
+// validatePartialPreemptionCreate validates a Job that opts into partial preemption via the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption="true" annotation. Partial preemption reclaims quota
+// reactively: Kueue evicts the Job's excess Pods and relies on the Job controller to recreate
+// them gated, rather than patching the Job. For batch/Job that is only safe when an evicted Pod
+// neither counts toward backoffLimit nor, on a clean SIGTERM exit, counts as a completion, so the
+// Job must use Indexed completion, restartPolicy Never, and a podFailurePolicy rule that ignores
+// the DisruptionTarget condition. The checks apply only when the PartialPreemption gate is on.
+func validatePartialPreemptionCreate(job *Job) field.ErrorList {
+	if !features.Enabled(features.PartialPreemption) ||
+		job.Annotations[constants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation] != "true" {
+		return nil
+	}
+	var allErrs field.ErrorList
+	if !workloadslicing.Enabled(job.Object()) {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(partialPreemptionAnnotationPath, "true",
+			fmt.Sprintf("partial preemption requires the elastic job annotation %s", constants.ElasticJobAnnotation)))
+	}
+	if ptr.Deref(job.Spec.CompletionMode, batchv1.NonIndexedCompletion) != batchv1.IndexedCompletion {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(completionModePath, ptr.Deref(job.Spec.CompletionMode, batchv1.NonIndexedCompletion),
+			"must be Indexed when partial preemption is enabled"))
+	}
+	if job.Spec.Template.Spec.RestartPolicy != corev1.RestartPolicyNever {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(restartPolicyPath, job.Spec.Template.Spec.RestartPolicy,
+			"must be Never when partial preemption is enabled"))
+	}
+	if !hasDisruptionTargetIgnoreRule(job.Spec.PodFailurePolicy) {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Required(podFailurePolicyPath,
+			fmt.Sprintf("must contain a rule with action %q ignoring the %q pod condition when partial preemption is enabled",
+				batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore, corev1.DisruptionTarget)))
+	}
+	return allErrs
+}
+
+// hasDisruptionTargetIgnoreRule reports whether the podFailurePolicy has a rule that ignores Pods
+// carrying a DisruptionTarget=True condition, so Kueue-evicted Pods do not count toward the Job's
+// backoffLimit.

+	if policy == nil {
+		return false
+	}
+	for _, rule := range policy.Rules {
```

  We only require that a rule ignoring DisruptionTarget=True exists — that is the minimum for reclaiming Pods. Rule order is up to the user: if they put FailJob first, reclaiming a Pod fails the Job, which just releases all the quota like a full preemption.

```diff
+// podSetPreemptionMinCount returns the partial-preemption scale-down floor read from the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption-min-count annotation, or nil when the feature gate is off

+func (j *Job) podSetPreemptionMinCount() *int32 {

+	}
+	strVal, found := j.GetAnnotations()[PartialPreemptionMinCountAnnotation]
+	if !found {
+		return nil
+	}
```

  `@zhengchenyu`
  
  _Kueue Development (context canary, see AGENTS.md)_
  
  CodeRabbit’s AI review found one remaining case: `validateUpdateForPartialPreemptionAnnotation` permits opt-in changes while `PartialPreemption` is disabled.
  
  If a Job opts out while the gate is disabled, its existing compatible Workload can retain the `"true"` annotation. After the gate is enabled again, the scheduler may partially preempt that opted-out Job.
  
  Please handle this gate transition and add a regression test. I’m not marking the finding as addressed yet.

```diff
+package job
+
+import (
+	"context"
+	"fmt"
+	"strconv"
+	"strings"
+
+	"github.com/onsi/ginkgo/v2"
+	"github.com/onsi/gomega"

+	corev1 "k8s.io/api/core/v1"

+	metav1 "k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/client"
+
+	kueue "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/apis/kueue/v1beta2"
+	pkgconstants "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/constants"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/features"
+	utilpod "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/util/pod"
+	utiltestingapi "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/util/testing/v1beta2"
+	testingjob "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/util/testingjobs/job"
+	testingpod "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/util/testingjobs/pod"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/workload"
+	workloadfinish "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/workload/finish"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/workloadslicing"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/test/integration/framework"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/test/util/behavioral"
+)
+
+// mintPod simulates a completion index; draining removes the highest indexes first.
+func mintPod(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix, workloadName string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, index int32) {
+	ginkgo.GinkgoHelper()
+	pod := testingpod.MakePod(fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", podNamePrefix, index), namespace).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Label(pkgconstants.PodSetLabel, string(podSet)).
+		Label(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Gate(kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate).
+		Obj()
+	behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, pod)
+}
+
+func expectPodCounts(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, wantUngated, wantGated int) {
+	ginkgo.GinkgoHelper()
+	gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+		pods := &corev1.PodList{}
+		g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, pods, client.InNamespace(namespace),
+			client.MatchingLabels{pkgconstants.PodSetLabel: string(podSet)})).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		ungated, gated := 0, 0
+		for i := range pods.Items {
+			if !strings.HasPrefix(pods.Items[i].Name, podNamePrefix+"-") {
+				continue
+			}
+			if utilpod.HasGate(&pods.Items[i], kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate) {
+				gated++
+			} else {
+				ungated++
+			}
+		}
+		g.Expect(ungated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantUngated))
+		g.Expect(gated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantGated))
+	}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+}
+
+// This exercises the full reactive partial-preemption loop for batch/Job, end to end:
+//
+//	request  - the scheduler sets partialPreemption.retainedCount when the preemptor arrives,
+//	drain    - the Job reconciler evicts the victim's excess Pods down to the target,
+//	release  - the ungater lowers the admitted count and flips PartiallyPreempted to QuotaReleased,
+//	admit    - the freed quota admits the preemptor,
+//	re-grow  - once the preemptor leaves, the victim scales back up to its original parallelism.
+var _ = ginkgo.Describe("Job partial preemption", ginkgo.Ordered, ginkgo.ContinueOnFailure, func() {
+	var (
+		ns *corev1.Namespace
+		rf *kueue.ResourceFlavor
+		cq *kueue.ClusterQueue
+		lq *kueue.LocalQueue
+	)
+
+	ginkgo.BeforeAll(func() {
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.ElasticJobsViaWorkloadSlices, true)
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.ReclaimablePods, true)
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.PartialPreemption, true)
+		fwk.StartManager(ctx, cfg, managerAndControllersSetup(false, true, nil))
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.AfterAll(func() {
+		fwk.StopManager(ctx)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.BeforeEach(func() {
+		ns = behavioral.CreateNamespaceFromPrefixWithLog(ctx, k8sClient, "job-partial-preemption-")
+
+		rf = utiltestingapi.MakeResourceFlavor("default").Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, rf)
+
+		cq = utiltestingapi.MakeClusterQueue("job-partial-preemption-cq").
+			ResourceGroup(*utiltestingapi.MakeFlavorQuotas(rf.Name).Resource(corev1.ResourceCPU, "4").Obj()).
+			Preemption(kueue.ClusterQueuePreemption{
+				WithinClusterQueue: kueue.PreemptionPolicyLowerPriority,
+			}).
+			Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, cq)
+
+		lq = utiltestingapi.MakeLocalQueue("job-partial-preemption-lq", ns.Name).ClusterQueue(cq.Name).Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, lq)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.AfterEach(func() {
+		gomega.Expect(behavioral.DeleteNamespace(ctx, k8sClient, ns)).To(gomega.Succeed())
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, cq, true)
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, lq, true)
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, rf, true)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.It("shrinks the victim to admit a preemptor, then re-grows it once the preemptor leaves", framework.SlowSpec, func() {
+		const parallelism int32 = 4
+		// The preemptor needs 2 CPU from the 4 CPU ClusterQueue the victim fully occupies, so the
+		// reclaim target is deterministic: parallelism (4) minus the preemptor's 2 CPU = 2.
+		const target int32 = 2
+
+		victim := testingjob.MakeJob("victim", ns.Name).
+			Queue(kueue.LocalQueueName(lq.Name)).
+			SetAnnotation(workloadslicing.EnabledAnnotationKey, workloadslicing.EnabledAnnotationValue).
+			SetAnnotation(pkgconstants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation, "true").
+			PodAnnotation(pkgconstants.PodSetPartialPreemptionMinRetainedCountAnnotation, "1").
+			Indexed(true).
+			Request(corev1.ResourceCPU, "1").
+			Parallelism(parallelism).
+			Completions(parallelism).
+			Obj()
+		victim.Spec.PodFailurePolicy = &batchv1.PodFailurePolicy{
+			Rules: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyRule{{
+				Action: batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore,
+				OnPodConditions: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyOnPodConditionsPattern{{
+					Type:   corev1.DisruptionTarget,
+					Status: corev1.ConditionTrue,
+				}},
+			}},
+		}
+
+		var (
+			victimWlKey client.ObjectKey
+			podSet      kueue.PodSetReference
+		)
+		ginkgo.By("admitting the victim that occupies the whole ClusterQueue", func() {
+			behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, victim)
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				workloads := &kueue.WorkloadList{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, workloads, client.InNamespace(ns.Name))).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				g.Expect(workloads.Items).Should(gomega.HaveLen(1))
+				wl := &workloads.Items[0]
+				g.Expect(workload.IsAdmitted(wl)).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments).Should(gomega.HaveLen(1))
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[0].Count).Should(gomega.HaveValue(gomega.Equal(parallelism)))
+				victimWlKey = client.ObjectKeyFromObject(wl)
+				podSet = wl.Spec.PodSets[0].Name
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("simulating the Job's running Pods and letting the ungater ungate them", func() {
+			for i := range parallelism {
+				mintPod(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", victimWlKey.Name, podSet, i)
+			}
+			expectPodCounts(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", podSet, int(parallelism), 0)
+		})
+
+		preemptor := utiltestingapi.MakeWorkload("preemptor", ns.Name).
+			Priority(10).
+			Queue(kueue.LocalQueueName(lq.Name)).
+			PodSets(*utiltestingapi.MakePodSet("main", 2).Request(corev1.ResourceCPU, "1").Obj()).
+			Obj()
+
+		ginkgo.By("requesting a partial preemption when the preemptor arrives", func() {
+			behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, preemptor)
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				wl := &kueue.Workload{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.Get(ctx, victimWlKey, wl)).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				got, ok := workload.PreemptionRetainedCount(wl, podSet)
+				g.Expect(ok).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
+				g.Expect(got).Should(gomega.Equal(target))
+				cond := meta.FindStatusCondition(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted)
+				g.Expect(cond).ShouldNot(gomega.BeNil())
+				g.Expect(cond.Status).Should(gomega.Equal(metav1.ConditionTrue))
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("draining the victim's excess Pods down to the target", func() {
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				pods := &corev1.PodList{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, pods, client.InNamespace(ns.Name),
+					client.MatchingLabels{pkgconstants.PodSetLabel: string(podSet)})).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				g.Expect(pods.Items).Should(gomega.HaveLen(int(target)))
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("releasing the quota by lowering the admitted count once drained", func() {
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				wl := &kueue.Workload{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.Get(ctx, victimWlKey, wl)).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				cond := meta.FindStatusCondition(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted)
+				g.Expect(cond).ShouldNot(gomega.BeNil())
+				g.Expect(cond.Status).Should(gomega.Equal(metav1.ConditionFalse))
+				g.Expect(cond.Reason).Should(gomega.Equal(kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreemptedReasonQuotaReleased))
+				// Reactive downscaling releases quota by lowering the admitted count to the target
+				// (not by reporting reclaimablePods). The reclaim target is no longer cleared once
+				// satisfied; the release is observable as the used count falling to it.
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[0].Count).Should(gomega.HaveValue(gomega.Equal(target)))
+				retained, hasTarget := workload.PreemptionRetainedCount(wl, podSet)
+				g.Expect(hasTarget).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
```

```diff
+// mintPod simulates a completion index; draining removes the highest indexes first.
+func mintPod(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix, workloadName string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, index int32) {
+	ginkgo.GinkgoHelper()
+	pod := testingpod.MakePod(fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", podNamePrefix, index), namespace).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(kueue.WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation, workloadName).
+		Annotation(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Label(pkgconstants.PodSetLabel, string(podSet)).
+		Label(batchv1.JobCompletionIndexAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(index))).
+		Gate(kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate).
+		Obj()
+	behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, pod)
+}
+
+func expectPodCounts(ctx context.Context, namespace, podNamePrefix string, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, wantUngated, wantGated int) {
+	ginkgo.GinkgoHelper()
+	gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+		pods := &corev1.PodList{}
+		g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, pods, client.InNamespace(namespace),
+			client.MatchingLabels{pkgconstants.PodSetLabel: string(podSet)})).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		ungated, gated := 0, 0
+		for i := range pods.Items {
+			if !strings.HasPrefix(pods.Items[i].Name, podNamePrefix+"-") {
+				continue
+			}
+			if utilpod.HasGate(&pods.Items[i], kueue.ElasticJobSchedulingGate) {
+				gated++
+			} else {
+				ungated++
+			}
+		}
+		g.Expect(ungated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantUngated))
+		g.Expect(gated).Should(gomega.Equal(wantGated))
+	}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+}
+
+// This exercises the full reactive partial-preemption loop for batch/Job, end to end:
+//
+//	request  - the scheduler sets partialPreemption.retainedCount when the preemptor arrives,
+//	drain    - the Job reconciler evicts the victim's excess Pods down to the target,
+//	release  - the ungater lowers the admitted count and flips PartiallyPreempted to QuotaReleased,
+//	admit    - the freed quota admits the preemptor,
+//	re-grow  - once the preemptor leaves, the victim scales back up to its original parallelism.
+var _ = ginkgo.Describe("Job partial preemption", ginkgo.Ordered, ginkgo.ContinueOnFailure, func() {
+	var (
+		ns *corev1.Namespace
+		rf *kueue.ResourceFlavor
+		cq *kueue.ClusterQueue
+		lq *kueue.LocalQueue
+	)
+
+	ginkgo.BeforeAll(func() {
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.ElasticJobsViaWorkloadSlices, true)
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.ReclaimablePods, true)
+		features.SetFeatureGateDuringTest(ginkgo.GinkgoTB(), features.PartialPreemption, true)
+		fwk.StartManager(ctx, cfg, managerAndControllersSetup(false, true, nil))
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.AfterAll(func() {
+		fwk.StopManager(ctx)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.BeforeEach(func() {
+		ns = behavioral.CreateNamespaceFromPrefixWithLog(ctx, k8sClient, "job-partial-preemption-")
+
+		rf = utiltestingapi.MakeResourceFlavor("default").Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, rf)
+
+		cq = utiltestingapi.MakeClusterQueue("job-partial-preemption-cq").
+			ResourceGroup(*utiltestingapi.MakeFlavorQuotas(rf.Name).Resource(corev1.ResourceCPU, "4").Obj()).
+			Preemption(kueue.ClusterQueuePreemption{
+				WithinClusterQueue: kueue.PreemptionPolicyLowerPriority,
+			}).
+			Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, cq)
+
+		lq = utiltestingapi.MakeLocalQueue("job-partial-preemption-lq", ns.Name).ClusterQueue(cq.Name).Obj()
+		behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, lq)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.AfterEach(func() {
+		gomega.Expect(behavioral.DeleteNamespace(ctx, k8sClient, ns)).To(gomega.Succeed())
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, cq, true)
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, lq, true)
+		behavioral.ExpectObjectToBeDeleted(ctx, k8sClient, rf, true)
+	})
+
+	ginkgo.It("shrinks the victim to admit a preemptor, then re-grows it once the preemptor leaves", framework.SlowSpec, func() {
+		const parallelism int32 = 4
+		// The preemptor needs 2 CPU from the 4 CPU ClusterQueue the victim fully occupies, so the
+		// reclaim target is deterministic: parallelism (4) minus the preemptor's 2 CPU = 2.
+		const target int32 = 2
+
+		victim := testingjob.MakeJob("victim", ns.Name).
+			Queue(kueue.LocalQueueName(lq.Name)).
+			SetAnnotation(workloadslicing.EnabledAnnotationKey, workloadslicing.EnabledAnnotationValue).
+			SetAnnotation(pkgconstants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation, "true").
+			PodAnnotation(pkgconstants.PodSetPartialPreemptionMinRetainedCountAnnotation, "1").
+			Indexed(true).
+			Request(corev1.ResourceCPU, "1").
+			Parallelism(parallelism).
+			Completions(parallelism).
+			Obj()
+		victim.Spec.PodFailurePolicy = &batchv1.PodFailurePolicy{
+			Rules: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyRule{{
+				Action: batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore,
+				OnPodConditions: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyOnPodConditionsPattern{{
+					Type:   corev1.DisruptionTarget,
+					Status: corev1.ConditionTrue,
+				}},
+			}},
+		}
+
+		var (
+			victimWlKey client.ObjectKey
+			podSet      kueue.PodSetReference
+		)
+		ginkgo.By("admitting the victim that occupies the whole ClusterQueue", func() {
+			behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, victim)
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				workloads := &kueue.WorkloadList{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, workloads, client.InNamespace(ns.Name))).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				g.Expect(workloads.Items).Should(gomega.HaveLen(1))
+				wl := &workloads.Items[0]
+				g.Expect(workload.IsAdmitted(wl)).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments).Should(gomega.HaveLen(1))
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[0].Count).Should(gomega.HaveValue(gomega.Equal(parallelism)))
+				victimWlKey = client.ObjectKeyFromObject(wl)
+				podSet = wl.Spec.PodSets[0].Name
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("simulating the Job's running Pods and letting the ungater ungate them", func() {
+			for i := range parallelism {
+				mintPod(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", victimWlKey.Name, podSet, i)
+			}
+			expectPodCounts(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", podSet, int(parallelism), 0)
+		})
+
+		preemptor := utiltestingapi.MakeWorkload("preemptor", ns.Name).
+			Priority(10).
+			Queue(kueue.LocalQueueName(lq.Name)).
+			PodSets(*utiltestingapi.MakePodSet("main", 2).Request(corev1.ResourceCPU, "1").Obj()).
+			Obj()
+
+		ginkgo.By("requesting a partial preemption when the preemptor arrives", func() {
+			behavioral.MustCreate(ctx, k8sClient, preemptor)
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				wl := &kueue.Workload{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.Get(ctx, victimWlKey, wl)).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				got, ok := workload.PreemptionRetainedCount(wl, podSet)
+				g.Expect(ok).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
+				g.Expect(got).Should(gomega.Equal(target))
+				cond := meta.FindStatusCondition(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted)
+				g.Expect(cond).ShouldNot(gomega.BeNil())
+				g.Expect(cond.Status).Should(gomega.Equal(metav1.ConditionTrue))
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("draining the victim's excess Pods down to the target", func() {
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				pods := &corev1.PodList{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, pods, client.InNamespace(ns.Name),
+					client.MatchingLabels{pkgconstants.PodSetLabel: string(podSet)})).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				g.Expect(pods.Items).Should(gomega.HaveLen(int(target)))
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("releasing the quota by lowering the admitted count once drained", func() {
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				wl := &kueue.Workload{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.Get(ctx, victimWlKey, wl)).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				cond := meta.FindStatusCondition(wl.Status.Conditions, kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreempted)
+				g.Expect(cond).ShouldNot(gomega.BeNil())
+				g.Expect(cond.Status).Should(gomega.Equal(metav1.ConditionFalse))
+				g.Expect(cond.Reason).Should(gomega.Equal(kueue.WorkloadPartiallyPreemptedReasonQuotaReleased))
+				// Reactive downscaling releases quota by lowering the admitted count to the target
+				// (not by reporting reclaimablePods). The reclaim target is no longer cleared once
+				// satisfied; the release is observable as the used count falling to it.
+				g.Expect(wl.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[0].Count).Should(gomega.HaveValue(gomega.Equal(target)))
+				retained, hasTarget := workload.PreemptionRetainedCount(wl, podSet)
+				g.Expect(hasTarget).Should(gomega.BeTrue())
+				used := workload.ExtractGrantedPodSetCounts(wl)[podSet]
+				g.Expect(used).Should(gomega.BeNumerically("<=", retained))
+			}, behavioral.Timeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("admitting the preemptor with the released quota", func() {
+			behavioral.ExpectWorkloadsToBeAdmitted(ctx, k8sClient, preemptor)
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("keeping the re-created Pods gated while the preemptor still holds the quota", func() {
+			// The drain deleted the highest indexes (target..parallelism-1). Re-create them: with the
+			// admitted count still at the target, the ungater caps ungating at the granted count, so
+			// the re-created Pods stay gated until the slice actually re-grows.
+			for i := target; i < parallelism; i++ {
+				mintPod(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", victimWlKey.Name, podSet, i)
+			}
+			// The low indexes keep running; the re-created high indexes stay gated.
+			expectPodCounts(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", podSet, int(target), int(parallelism-target))
+		})
+
+		ginkgo.By("re-growing the victim back to its original parallelism once the preemptor leaves", func() {
+			// Deleting the preemptor returns its quota to the ClusterQueue. The Job reconciler sees
+			// spec.parallelism (4) above the admitted count (target) and creates a scale-up Workload
+			// slice, which the scheduler admits against the freed quota, replacing the shrunk slice.
+			gomega.Expect(k8sClient.Delete(ctx, preemptor)).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+
+			// The scale-up slice replaces the shrunk one: the old slice is left Finished (and lingers
+			// briefly), so select the victim's live admitted slice by skipping Finished Workloads.
+			gomega.Eventually(func(g gomega.Gomega) {
+				workloads := &kueue.WorkloadList{}
+				g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, workloads, client.InNamespace(ns.Name))).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+				var admitted *kueue.Workload
+				for i := range workloads.Items {
+					wl := &workloads.Items[i]
+					if metav1.IsControlledBy(wl, victim) && workload.IsAdmitted(wl) && !workloadfinish.IsFinished(wl) {
+						admitted = wl
+					}
+				}
+				g.Expect(admitted).ShouldNot(gomega.BeNil())
+				g.Expect(admitted.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments).Should(gomega.HaveLen(1))
+				g.Expect(admitted.Status.Admission.PodSetAssignments[0].Count).Should(gomega.HaveValue(gomega.Equal(parallelism)))
+				g.Expect(workload.ExtractGrantedPodSetCounts(admitted)[podSet]).Should(gomega.Equal(parallelism))
+			}, behavioral.MediumTimeout, behavioral.Interval).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+
+			expectPodCounts(ctx, ns.Name, "victim", podSet, int(parallelism), 0)
+		})
+	})
+
+	// This reproduces the band-wise over-shedding scenario from a real cluster (KEP-975 Case 1,
+	// step 2), reusing the exact same environment and reactive steps as the single-victim test
+	// above, only with two equal-priority elastic victims instead of one.
+	//
+	// low-01 and low-02 (parallelism 2, minCount 1, created in that order) fill the 4 CPU
+	// ClusterQueue. A higher-priority preemptor needs 3 CPU. Band-wise reclaim must:
+	//
+	//	shrink both low victims to their floor (low-01 2->1, low-02 2->1) which frees 2, still 1
+	//	short, so the newest victim is evicted whole (low-02 1->0) which frees the last 1.
+	//
+	// Expected end state: preemptor admitted, low-01 running at 1 (its floor), low-02 fully
+	// evicted. The bug evicts BOTH low victims instead.
+	ginkgo.It("reclaims band-wise: shrinks both low victims to the floor, then evicts only the newest", framework.SlowSpec, func() {
+		const parallelism int32 = 2
+		const minCount int32 = 1
+		const floor int32 = 1
+
+		makeLowJob := func(name string) *batchv1.Job {
+			job := testingjob.MakeJob(name, ns.Name).
+				Queue(kueue.LocalQueueName(lq.Name)).

+				SetAnnotation(pkgconstants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation, "true").
+				PodAnnotation(pkgconstants.PodSetPartialPreemptionMinRetainedCountAnnotation, strconv.Itoa(int(minCount))).
+				Indexed(true).
+				Request(corev1.ResourceCPU, "1").
+				Parallelism(parallelism).
+				Completions(parallelism).
+				Obj()
+			job.Spec.PodFailurePolicy = &batchv1.PodFailurePolicy{
+				Rules: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyRule{{
+					Action: batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore,
+					OnPodConditions: []batchv1.PodFailurePolicyOnPodConditionsPattern{{
+						Type:   corev1.DisruptionTarget,
+						Status: corev1.ConditionTrue,
+					}},
+				}},
+			}
+			return job
+		}
+
+		// liveAdmittedSlice returns the job's current (non-finished) admitted Workload slice.
+		liveAdmittedSlice := func(g gomega.Gomega, jobName string) *kueue.Workload {
+			workloads := &kueue.WorkloadList{}
+			g.Expect(k8sClient.List(ctx, workloads, client.InNamespace(ns.Name))).Should(gomega.Succeed())
+			var live *kueue.Workload
+			for i := range workloads.Items {
+				wl := &workloads.Items[i]
+				if len(wl.OwnerReferences) == 0 || wl.OwnerReferences[0].Name != jobName {
+					continue
+				}
+				if workload.IsAdmitted(wl) && !workloadfinish.IsFinished(wl) {
+					live = wl
+				}
+			}
+			return live
+		}
```

  This change is outside of the FG, so I would like to revert it. We shouldn't really be using loose mode from other places than scheduler. We only use loose from scheduler because it is very latency sensitive, and only in limited cases.

```diff
+
+// plan drives how a single strategy's candidates are consumed and backfilled.
+// walk plans each candidate into a target and applies it to the snapshot through
+// the yield it is given; fillBack then restores whatever the preemptor no longer
+// needs.
+type plan interface {
+	// walk plans every candidate and yields it.
+	walk() iter.Seq[*Target]
+	// fillBack restores, in reverse order, every victim the preemptor no longer
+	// needs and returns the surviving targets in the order the candidates were
+	// considered (decreasing preemption appeal).
+	fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target
+}
+
+// fullPlan whole-evicts every candidate in a single pass. It is used directly when
+// the PartialPreemption feature is off.
+type fullPlan struct {
```

  I was wondering on optimizing two aspects for the solution:
  1. unify fullPlan  and partialPlan to make sure they behave the same when PartialPreeption is off
  2. minimize changes to fairSharing and classical preemption to make sure we have only one algorithm
  
  After a couple of iterations with AI agents I arrived at: https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/commit/094b73e53e28a473d3ce8998cd9a7de2f424592b
  
  Side benefit is that it works for fair sharing ooo. Also, the diff against mail is smaller.
  
  This one memorizes the victims as they are consume rather than when outputing them. Feel free to rework the code,  or use it as is.

  I like this a lot more. The strategies just pick the order now, and all the partial logic is in one place behind the gate, so classical and FS don't fork. 
  One thing: the plan walks the candidates twice and expects them to re-read the snapshot. Could we say that on PreemptionStrategy? Right now it's only in the partialPlan comment. +func (p *fullPlan) walk() iter.Seq[*Target] {

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  **[P2] Preserve configurable victims and restore their snapshot usage.** With both `ConfigurablePreemptions` and `PartialPreemption` enabled, the evaluator removes each rule victim before returning it. `currentCandidate` then finds no snapshot entry, and this branch drops the victim without recording a restoration target. In the existing Always/WithinClusterQueue fixture (two admitted 1-CPU Workloads, quota 2, incoming request 1), partial preemption off returns whole target `a1` and restores both Workloads and 2 CPU. With it on, no targets are returned and the snapshot retains zero Workloads and zero usage. Please preserve ordinary rule-selected whole eviction and restore the snapshot on failure. This probe establishes candidate loss and a snapshot leak, not live oversubscription.

```diff
+// plan drives how a single strategy's candidates are consumed and backfilled.

+// the yield it is given; fillBack then restores whatever the preemptor no longer

+	// needs and returns the surviving targets in the order the candidates were
+	// considered (decreasing preemption appeal).

+}
+
+// fullPlan whole-evicts every candidate in a single pass. It is used directly when

+type fullPlan struct {
+	pCtx           *preemptionCtx
+	iterator       candidateIterator
+	allowBorrowing bool
+}
+
+func (p *fullPlan) walk() iter.Seq[*Target] {
+	return func(yield func(*Target) bool) {
+		defer p.iterator.Close()
+		yield = policy.YieldFromSnapshot(p.pCtx.snapshot, yield)
+		p.iterator.Reset()

+		}
+	}
+}
+
+func (p *fullPlan) fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target {
+	// In the reverse order, check if any of the workloads can be added back.

+		p.pCtx.snapshot.AddWorkload(targets[i].WorkloadInfo)

+		} else {
+			p.pCtx.snapshot.RemoveWorkload(targets[i].WorkloadInfo)

+}

+// partialPlan shrinks shrinkable victims to their floor first and, only if that is

+type partialPlan struct {
+	pCtx           *preemptionCtx

+	allowBorrowing bool
+	// ordering sorts victims by decreasing preemption appeal; fillBack uses it to
+	// restore the least appealing (highest priority) victims first.
+	ordering func(a, b *workload.Info) int
+}
+
+// walk consumes the candidates in three iterator passes covering four phases, ordered
+// from the least to the most disruptive:
+//
+//   - Pass 1 starts in the evicted phase: the already-evicted prefix (the candidates
+//     the generator emits first) is planned as a whole eviction. At the first
+//     non-evicted candidate the phase switches to pending, and from there only
+//     in-flight partial victims are reused/extended.
+//   - Pass 2 is the partial phase: every remaining shrinkable victim is shrunk to its
+//     floor. planPreemption returns a partial target only when the live used count is
+//     still above the floor, so victims already at their floor (the pending ones) and
+//     non-shrinkable ones are skipped.
+//   - Pass 3 is the full phase: everything still present is escalated to a whole
+//     eviction. currentCandidate skips the evicted prefix because it is no longer in
+//     the snapshot.
+//
+// Every phase reads the victim's live state from the snapshot, so a victim already
+// shrunk in an earlier phase is planned against its reduced instance. A shrinkable
+// victim therefore appears twice in the yielded stream: once as a pass-2 partial and
+// once as a pass-3 whole eviction; fillBack merges the two stages.
+func (p *partialPlan) walk() iter.Seq[*Target] {
+	return func(yield func(*Target) bool) {
+		defer p.iterator.Close()
+		yield = policy.YieldFromSnapshot(p.pCtx.snapshot, yield)
+		// Pass 1: the already-evicted prefix as whole evictions, then the in-flight
+		// partial victims reused/extended.
+		inEvictedPrefix := true
+		p.iterator.Reset()
+		for {
+			t := p.iterator.Next(p.allowBorrowing)
+			if t == nil {
+				break
+			}
+			cq, current := currentCandidate(p.pCtx, t.WorkloadInfo)
+			if current == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			if inEvictedPrefix {
+				if workloadevict.IsEvicted(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj) {
+					if !yield(wholeTarget(t, cq)) {
+						return
+					}
+					continue
+				}
+				// The evicted prefix is exhausted; the rest of this pass reuses pending victims.
+				inEvictedPrefix = false
+			}
+			// Pass 1 only reuses victims that already have an in-flight partial-preemption
+			// request; other non-evicted candidates are left to pass 2.
+			if !workload.HasPendingPreemptionRetainedCount(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj) {
+				continue
+			}
+			target := planPreemption(t, current)
+			if target == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			target.WorkloadCq = cq
+			if !yield(target) {
+				return
+			}
+		}
+		// Pass 2: partial phase.
+		p.iterator.Reset()
+		for {
+			t := p.iterator.Next(p.allowBorrowing)
+			if t == nil {
+				break
+			}
+			cq, current := currentCandidate(p.pCtx, t.WorkloadInfo)
+			if current == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			target := planPreemption(t, current)
+			if target == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			target.WorkloadCq = cq
+			if !yield(target) {
+				return
+			}
+		}
+		// Pass 3: full phase. Escalate the still-present candidates to whole evictions.
+		p.iterator.Reset()
+		for {
+			t := p.iterator.Next(p.allowBorrowing)
+			if t == nil {
+				break
+			}
+			cq, current := currentCandidate(p.pCtx, t.WorkloadInfo)
+			if current == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			if !yield(wholeTarget(t, cq)) {
+				return
+			}
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+// fillBack merges the staged targets produced by walk and then backfills them in
+// reverse (least appealing, i.e. highest priority, victim restored first).
+//
+// A shrinkable victim can appear twice in the stream: once shrunk (a partial stage)
+// and once escalated to a whole eviction. mergeStagedTargets collapses each victim to
+// a single target and re-sorts the result by preemption appeal, so backfill does not
+// rely on the order the stages happened to be yielded in.
+//
+// The merged list is walked in reverse. A whole eviction is restored and dropped if the
+// preemptor still fits, otherwise kept. A shrinkable victim is handled by
+// fillbackPartialPreemption, which tries, in order, a full restore, a floor restore,
+// and finally a search for the largest count above the floor that still fits.
+func (p *partialPlan) fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target {
+	merged := p.mergeStagedTargets(targets)
+	for i := len(merged) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
+		t := merged[i]
+		// A shrinkable victim, whether staged as a partial shrink or escalated to a whole
+		// eviction, is grown back over the full {0} ∪ [min, high] range by
+		// fillbackPartialPreemption. The branch keys on whether the victim is shrinkable,
+		// not on the staged target kind, so an escalated victim still gets its partial
+		// range searched instead of a plain restore-or-keep.
+		if podSet, ok := workload.PartialPreemptionPodSet(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj, t.WorkloadInfo.TotalRequests); ok {
+			if fillbackPartialPreemption(ctx, p.pCtx, t, podSet, p.allowBorrowing) {
+				// O(1) deletion: copy the last element into index i and reduce size.
+				merged[i] = merged[len(merged)-1]
+				merged = merged[:len(merged)-1]
+			}
+			continue
+		}
+		// Whole eviction: restore it and drop it if the preemptor still fits.
+		p.pCtx.snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+		if workloadFits(ctx, p.pCtx, p.allowBorrowing) {
+			merged[i] = merged[len(merged)-1]
+			merged = merged[:len(merged)-1]
+			continue
+		}
+		p.pCtx.snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	}
+	return merged
+}
+
+// mergeStagedTargets collapses the staged targets to one target per victim and sorts
+// them by decreasing preemption appeal. A shrinkable victim that both shrank (pass 2)
+// and later escalated to a whole eviction (pass 3) appears twice; the first staged target
+// is kept. Both stages carry the same full WorkloadInfo, and fillbackPartialPreemption
+// reconstructs the victim's walk-end state from the live snapshot (see wasWholeEvicted)
+// rather than from the target, so the outcome is identical whichever stage is kept. The
+// result is sorted by p.ordering so fillBack can restore the least appealing victims first
+// regardless of the yielded stage order.
+func (p *partialPlan) mergeStagedTargets(targets []*Target) []*Target {
+	byKey := make(map[workload.Reference]*Target, len(targets))
+	for _, t := range targets {
+		key := workload.Key(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj)
+		if _, seen := byKey[key]; !seen {
+			byKey[key] = t
+		}
+	}
+	merged := make([]*Target, 0, len(byKey))
+	for _, t := range byKey {
+		merged = append(merged, t)
+	}
+	slices.SortStableFunc(merged, func(a, b *Target) int {
+		return p.ordering(a.WorkloadInfo, b.WorkloadInfo)
+	})
+	return merged
+}
+
+// fillbackPartialPreemption grows a shrinkable victim t back toward its full size. It
+// tries, in order:
+//
+//	(a) a full restore — drop the target entirely if the preemptor still fits with the
+//	    victim completely back;
+//	(b) if the full restore didn't apply or didn't fit, a restore to minRetainedCount
+//	    (the floor): if that fits, there's no need to fall back further. If even
+//	    minRetainedCount doesn't fit, the outcome depends on how walk left the victim,
+//	    captured as wasWholeEvicted before the snapshot is mutated: a victim already
+//	    whole-evicted during walk settles at 0, which walk already proved fits; a
+//	    victim that was only shrunk to minRetainedCount (never escalated) keeps that
+//	    shrink instead of escalating to a whole eviction here — its PartialPreemption
+//	    already reflects minRetainedCount, set by pass 1/2's planPreemption, so there is
+//	    nothing left to update;
+//	(c) if minRetainedCount fits, a search in (minRetainedCount, min(full, high)) for the
+//	    largest count that still fits (high caps the regrowth at an already in-flight
+//	    retained count, if one is pending for this victim).
+//
+// All arithmetic uses the original full WorkloadInfo so Revert/Apply restore the
+// victim's whole quota.
+func fillbackPartialPreemption(ctx context.Context, preemptionCtx *preemptionCtx, t *Target, podSet kueue.PodSetReference, allowBorrowing bool) (dropped bool) {
+	minRetainedCount, _ := workload.PreemptionMinRetainedCount(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj, podSet)
+	full := podSetRequestCount(t.WorkloadInfo, podSet)
+	high := full
+	if pending, has := workload.PreemptionRetainedCount(t.WorkloadInfo.Obj, podSet); has && pending < high {
+		high = pending
+	}
+

+			snapshot.RemoveWorkload(current)
+		}

+			snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
```

  > [!NOTE]
  > 🤖 **GPT-6.1 Sol responding on behalf of @vladikkuzn**
  
  **[P2] Retain the other PodSets when the shrinkable count reaches zero.** `setTo(0)` removes the entire Workload during fit checks. For a generic elastic Workload with a 2-CPU head and eight 1-CPU workers (worker floor 0), quota 10 and incoming request 9, both classical iterators return workers retained at 0. Applying that target to a fresh snapshot keeps the 2-CPU head, so the incoming Workload does not fit. Planning also leaves CQ usage at 8 CPU while the restored Workload uses 10 CPU. Install the reduced instance at zero workers too, so the other PodSets remain charged. This is a generic planner fixture; no batch Job, API-server or live admission path was reproduced.

  The successful cohort-1 case is fixed. Two cases remain on this head:
  
  - With quota 6 CPU, an elastic victim using 5 CPU (floor 1, pending retained count 2), and incoming demand 7, both classical iterators return no targets but leave CQ usage at 9 CPU while the restored victim still uses 5 CPU. The no-fit path restores both the partial and whole stages of the same victim. Restoration needs to recover the original membership and usage once per victim.
  - The nominal-quota fallback remains missing. `TestPartialPreemptionFillBackCohort2Regression` currently passes by expecting whole eviction with the gate off and no targets with it on. For P nominal 8, A nominal 5/usage 10/floor 5, and incoming demand 11, shrinking A to nominal removes it from the whole pass. Please change that expectation and retry whole-only planning after restoring a failed partial attempt, as the merged KEP requires.
  
  These results come from planner/snapshot tests, not a live scheduling cycle.

```diff
+// addOrReplaceTarget commits target into the targets slice. If target's workload already
+// occupies a slot (recorded on a reduced copy from an earlier iterator round), the existing
+// entry is replaced in place and the original full workload.Info is preserved as the target's
+// WorkloadInfo, so restoreSnapshot and the eventual eviction act on the full workload. Otherwise
+// the target is appended and its slot is recorded on the reduced copy (if any) so a later round
+// can find it. Using the slot stored on workload.Info avoids a per-attempt map on this hot path.
+func addOrReplaceTarget(targets []*Target, target *Target) []*Target {
+	if idx, ok := target.WorkloadInfo.GetPreemptionTargetSlot(); ok {
+		target.WorkloadInfo = targets[idx].WorkloadInfo
+		targets[idx] = target
+		if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+			target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(idx)
+		}
+		return targets
+	}
+	targets = append(targets, target)
+	if target.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		target.ReducedInfo.SetPreemptionTargetSlot(len(targets) - 1)
+	}
+	return targets
+}
+
+// planPreemption decides how preempting candidate would proceed without committing it; it leaves the
+// snapshot unchanged. For an elastic candidate it shrinks the reclaimable PodSet by one replica,
+// bounded below by preemptionMinCount; otherwise, and with the gate off, it is a whole eviction.
+func planPreemption(candidate *workload.Info, reason string) *Target {
+	if ppInfo := partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate); ppInfo != nil {
+		reducedInfo := reducedInfoForPartialPreemption(candidate, ppInfo.podSet, ppInfo.targetCount)
+		return &Target{
+			WorkloadInfo: candidate,
+			Reason:       reason,
+			PartialPreemption: &policy.PartialPreemptionTarget{
+				PodSet:                ppInfo.podSet,
+				PreemptionTargetCount: ppInfo.targetCount,
+			},
+			ReducedInfo: reducedInfo,
+		}
+	}
+	// Whole eviction.
+	return &Target{WorkloadInfo: candidate, Reason: reason}
+}
+
+// applyPreemption simulates preempting the target in the snapshot: for a partial target it replaces
+// the full workload with its reduced (scaled-down) copy, freeing only the reclaimed replicas; for a
+// whole target it removes the workload entirely.
+func applyPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.AddWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+}
+
+// revertPreemption is the inverse of applyPreemption: it removes the reduced copy of a partial target
+// (if any) and adds the full workload back, restoring the snapshot to its pre-preemption state.
+func revertPreemption(snapshot *schdcache.Snapshot, t *Target) {
+	if t.ReducedInfo != nil {
+		snapshot.RemoveWorkload(t.ReducedInfo)
+	}
+	snapshot.AddWorkload(t.WorkloadInfo)
+}
+
+type partialPreemptionInfo struct {
+	podSet kueue.PodSetReference
+	// targetCount is this round's reclaim target: used-1 bounded below by preemptionMinCount, or a
+	// still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount reused directly.
+	targetCount int32
+}
+
+// partialPreemptionInfoFor returns this round's reclaim target for candidate, or nil to fall back to
+// whole eviction. A still-draining in-flight PreemptionTargetCount is reused directly; otherwise the
+// target is used-1 (the per-round cap) bounded below by preemptionMinCount. Eligible only when
+// exactly one PodSet is above its floor and has no topology assignment.
+func partialPreemptionInfoFor(candidate *workload.Info) *partialPreemptionInfo {
+	// An already-evicted victim has a whole eviction in flight; its full quota is being
+	// reclaimed. Re-planning it as a scale-down would under-count the freed quota and push
+	// the preemptor to evict another victim, causing cross-cycle oscillation.
+	if !candidate.IsPartialPreemptionJob || workloadevict.IsEvicted(candidate.Obj) {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	floors := make(map[kueue.PodSetReference]int32, len(candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets))
+	for i := range candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets {
+		ps := &candidate.Obj.Spec.PodSets[i]
+		if ps.PreemptionMinCount != nil {
+			floors[ps.Name] = *ps.PreemptionMinCount
+		}
+	}
+	if len(floors) == 0 {
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	var info *partialPreemptionInfo
+	for _, psr := range candidate.TotalRequests {
+		minCount, ok := floors[psr.Name]
+		if !ok {
+			continue
+		}
+		used := psr.Count
+		if used <= minCount {
+			// Already at or below its floor; this PodSet cannot shrink further.
+			continue
+		}
+		// TODO: TAS victims are not supported yet. A shrinkable PodSet with a topology
+		// assignment forces whole eviction.
+		if psr.TopologyRequest != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// At most one PodSet may shrink per candidate today; a second one above its floor
+		// falls back to whole eviction.
+		if info != nil {
+			return nil
+		}
+		// A pending reclaim target still draining: reuse it so the candidate stays preemptible while
+		// its Pods terminate.
+		if target, ok := workload.PreemptionTargetCount(candidate.Obj, psr.Name); ok && used > target {
+			info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+				podSet:      psr.Name,
+				targetCount: target,
+			}
+			continue
+		}
+		// Reclaim one replica this round (target used-1, bounded by preemptionMinCount); later rounds
+		// deepen it.
+		reclaimable := min(used-minCount, maxReclaimedReplicasPerRound)
+		info = &partialPreemptionInfo{
+			podSet:      psr.Name,
+			targetCount: used - reclaimable,
+		}
+	}
+	return info
+}
```

```diff
+// validatePartialPreemptionCreate validates a Job that opts into partial preemption via the
+// kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption="true" annotation. Partial preemption reclaims quota
+// reactively: Kueue evicts the Job's excess Pods and relies on the Job controller to recreate
+// them gated, rather than patching the Job. For batch/Job that is only safe when an evicted Pod
+// neither counts toward backoffLimit nor, on a clean SIGTERM exit, counts as a completion, so the
+// Job must use Indexed completion, restartPolicy Never, and a podFailurePolicy rule that ignores
+// the DisruptionTarget condition. The checks apply only when the PartialPreemption gate is on.
+func validatePartialPreemptionCreate(job *Job) field.ErrorList {
+	if !features.Enabled(features.PartialPreemption) ||
+		job.Annotations[constants.PartialPreemptionAnnotation] != "true" {
+		return nil
+	}
+	var allErrs field.ErrorList
+	if !workloadslicing.Enabled(job.Object()) {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(partialPreemptionAnnotationPath, "true",
+			fmt.Sprintf("partial preemption requires the elastic job annotation %s", constants.ElasticJobAnnotation)))
+	}
+	if ptr.Deref(job.Spec.CompletionMode, batchv1.NonIndexedCompletion) != batchv1.IndexedCompletion {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(completionModePath, ptr.Deref(job.Spec.CompletionMode, batchv1.NonIndexedCompletion),
+			"must be Indexed when partial preemption is enabled"))
+	}
+	if job.Spec.Template.Spec.RestartPolicy != corev1.RestartPolicyNever {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(restartPolicyPath, job.Spec.Template.Spec.RestartPolicy,
+			"must be Never when partial preemption is enabled"))
+	}
+	if !hasDisruptionTargetIgnoreRule(job.Spec.PodFailurePolicy) {
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, field.Required(podFailurePolicyPath,
+			fmt.Sprintf("must contain a rule with action %q ignoring the %q pod condition when partial preemption is enabled",
+				batchv1.PodFailurePolicyActionIgnore, corev1.DisruptionTarget)))
+	}
+	return allErrs
+}

+	if policy == nil {

+	}
```

```diff
+// plan drives how a single strategy's candidates are consumed and backfilled.
+// walk plans each candidate into a target and applies it to the snapshot through
+// the yield it is given; fillBack then restores whatever the preemptor no longer
+// needs.
+type plan interface {
+	// walk plans every candidate and yields it.
+	walk() iter.Seq[*Target]
+	// fillBack restores, in reverse order, every victim the preemptor no longer
+	// needs and returns the surviving targets in the order the candidates were
+	// considered (decreasing preemption appeal).
+	fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target
+}

+type fullPlan struct {
```

  I’ve tried using the plan layer to introduce an abstraction between strategy and iterator, but it turns out to be challenging(for fair, not for classical). The main reasons are as follows:
  * The classical strategy can provide a candidate iterator, making it straightforward to perform multi-round iteration on top of the iterator to implement partial preemption.
  * For the fair strategy, candidate selection is coupled with YieldFromSnapshot to calculate the DRS for victim removal. Since we manipulate snapshots within the plan, this conflicts with the fair implementation. It will be difficult to support partial preemption for now.
  > How about unhold https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18851 and reopen this pr after fair is decoupled?
  
  I read [094b73e5](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/commit/094b73e53e28a473d3ce8998cd9a7de2f424592b), great idea! But has two issue:
  * (1) **candidate priority order**: shrink retreats every shrinkable victim to its floor before evict. That's fine today, but regardless of candidate priority order, we may need `shrink→evict→shrink→evict`.
  * (2) **pending partial not first**: candidates order [L1 evicted, L2 partial, L3 pending-partial], if L1 + L2 already fits, we will never touch L3. We should iterate in two rounds: the first round handles pending items, and the second round handles partial items.
  I think (2) is fine and can be fixed directly. As for (1), it works for the current design, but it is not a long-term final solution.
  
  Should we proceed based on [094b73e5](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/commit/094b73e53e28a473d3ce8998cd9a7de2f424592b), and then consider further modifications later?
  Alternatively, we can introduce a hacky switch: if partialPreempt=true and classical strategy is selected, route to the partialPlan path, and keep the existing logic for everything else.

```diff
+)
+
+// plan drives how a single strategy's candidates are consumed and backfilled.
+// walk plans each candidate into a target and applies it to the snapshot through
+// the yield it is given; fillBack then restores whatever the preemptor no longer
+// needs.
+type plan interface {
+	// walk plans every candidate and yields it.
+	walk() iter.Seq[*Target]
+	// fillBack restores, in reverse order, every victim the preemptor no longer
+	// needs and returns the surviving targets in the order the candidates were
+	// considered (decreasing preemption appeal).
+	fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target
+}
+
+// fullPlan whole-evicts every candidate in a single pass. It is used directly when
+// the PartialPreemption feature is off.
+type fullPlan struct {
```

  Yes, we plan to do that using the buckets within priority
  
  > As for (1), it works for the current design, but it is not a long-term final solution.
  
  Yes

- **pkg/scheduler/preemption/plan.go:48** by [@zhengchenyu](https://github.com/zhengchenyu) — 2026-11-18T12:35:12Z
```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,429 @@

+	"sort"
+

+	schdcache "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/cache/scheduler"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/features"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/scheduler/preemption/policy"
+	"sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/workload"
+	workloadevict "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/pkg/workload/evict"
+)
+
+// plan drives how a single strategy's candidates are consumed and backfilled.
+// walk plans each candidate into a target and applies it to the snapshot through
+// the yield it is given; fillBack then restores whatever the preemptor no longer
+// needs.
+type plan interface {
+	// walk plans every candidate and yields it.
+	walk() iter.Seq[*Target]
+	// fillBack restores, in reverse order, every victim the preemptor no longer
+	// needs and returns the surviving targets in the order the candidates were
+	// considered (decreasing preemption appeal).
+	fillBack(ctx context.Context, targets []*Target) []*Target
+}
+
+// fullPlan whole-evicts every candidate in a single pass. It is used directly when
+// the PartialPreemption feature is off.
+type fullPlan struct {
```

  Here’s my design idea: the plan sits between strategy and iterator:
  * The strategy selects fair, classical, or other higher-level policies.
  * The iterator is the candidate iterator.
  
  The plan serves as the intermediary layer between them:
  * If partial-preempt is disabled, the plan runs one round by default, namely fullPlan.
  * If partial-preempt is enabled, under the current design, the plan iterates over the iterator three times:
  
  The first pass handles `evicted` + `pending`, the second pass handles `partial`, and the third pass handles `evicted` — this is partialPlan.
  
  When priority (+hierarchical advantage) are involved, we only modify fullPlan, or introduce a new fullPlan, to adjust the second and third passes. We perform a Reset each time we advance to the next bucket.
  
  Maybe I should simplify this later, for example by avoiding the iterator in fair-strategy.go, to reduce impacts on other code.


  Yeah, I saw the idea in the PR, and this prompted me to change it. Specicially because it means maintaining two modes: fullPlan and partialPlan separately. I prefer to have one plan. then it is clear that the logic is the same.
  
   Actually, I think there were cases in your code where fullPlan and partialPlan differ when there are no workloads opting-in for the partial preemption - I will double check that, but generally having one plan makes sure there is no fork.


  Ok, I double checked the divergent semantics between fullPlan and partialPlan when no worklods opt-in. Here are the cases identified by my agent:
  
  1. With ConfigurablePreemptions also enabled:
  - Symptom: gate off finds the target; gate on returns no targets and leaves the ClusterQueue empty in the snapshot.
  - Cause: partialPlan.walk skips candidates that are no longer in the snapshot (currentCandidate returns nil). The configurable candidates are removed before they are offered, so they are neither targets nor restored.
  2. partialPlan.fillBack also tries to give back the last target; fullPlan.fillBack never does
  - Symptom: gate off returns wl2, gate on returns nothing.
  - Caveat: it only shows because that fixture's incoming workload has zero computed usage, so the fit check passes with nothing removed. I know of no production scenario for it; it is a structural difference, not a user-facing bug.
  
   These cases will be problematic to ever graduate the FG, there are probably corner cases for now, but I believe that if we fork the logic, the number of such cases will grow over time. So,  I prefer to have a design which avoids the possibility of forking the logic.


  Also, once we do that I would propose the following naming cleanups:
  - plan.go -> partial_preemption.go
  - shrink function -> shrinkPass
  - giveBack -> fillBack
  - set -> setKeep
  - memorize -> observe
  - finish -> buildTargets
  
  Then the top level getTargets will read:
  
  ```golang
  plan := newPartialPlan(strategy)
  if targets := plan.shrinkPass(ctx); targets != nil {
  	return targets
  }
  // ...
  if partial := plan.evictionPass(ctx, walked); partial != nil {
  	return partial
  }
  ```

```diff
+// fullPlan whole-evicts every candidate in a single pass. It is used directly when

+func (p *fullPlan) walk() iter.Seq[*Target] {

+		yield = policy.YieldFromSnapshot(p.pCtx.snapshot, yield)
+		p.iterator.Reset()
+		for {

+			if t == nil {
```
